# The work queue

CONDUCT owns this file and is its only writer. **Exception, 2026-07-31: session BOB
restructured it once, with Bob's explicit authorisation, while CONDUCT was paused for
that purpose.** Ownership returns to CONDUCT with this rewrite; BOB hands
decompositions over rather than editing here (`ORCHESTRATION.md`).

One section per area. An area is **ACTIVE** (holds a worker slot; max two at once) or
**DORMANT** (pre-seeded, promoted when a slot frees). CONDUCT takes the top item whose
status is `queued` and whose depends-on are all `done`, spawns a worker, and on
landing marks it `done`.

**What changed in this rewrite**, so CONDUCT can read it fresh without reconstructing:

- Every item now names its **milestone** (`MILESTONES.md`) and carries an
  **`accepts-when:`** — a command, not a judgment. That was `PLAN.md`'s one
  irreplaceable property and it was lost when the queue replaced it.
- The queue previously held six items while the real forward work was ~40 debt rows,
  six design-doc order-of-work lists and ten CONSTRUCTS steps. Everything is now
  placed in `MILESTONES.md`, and this file carries the runnable slice.
- **RECORD is a new area** (`PARALLELISM.md`): the store core and retrieval, ground
  already being edited with no owner, which is why everything defaulted to CAPTURE.
- **CPDF-2 is SUPERSEDED** by Bob's function-specific Worker topology (I6). Its work
  is not discarded — it becomes the pdf-worker's Tier 2 core.
- **CAP-2 landed** (39a0e1b) and is marked done here; CONDUCT paused before recording
  it.

**BOTH slots are CONTENT-OFFICE**, decided 2026-08-03: Bob confirmed office formats
as the current focus the same day he paused the case-making thread, and his
decomposition names COFF-1 ∥ COFF-2 as "the two behavioural slots" — disjoint files,
genuinely independent. Enacted 2026-08-03 by CONDUCT:

- **Slot 1 · CONTENT-OFFICE, COFF-1** — the FORMAT registry, HTML and PDF moved onto
  it. The D-70 uniformity test, and the step that must precede the format entries or
  they are built twice.
- **Slot 2 · CONTENT-OFFICE, COFF-2** — the OOXML container reader, a pure
  zero-dependency module. Builds against I7 on paper; independent of COFF-1's landing.
- Wave 2 is COFF-3/4/5 (mutually independent; two slots against three items —
  recommended order XLSX → DOCX → PPTX by evidentiary density; final sequencing is
  CONDUCT's). All three additionally wait on IC-1 RESOLVED — done 2026-08-03, see
  `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md`. Slot rotation 2026-08-03 as the wave drained: COFF-3 and
  COFF-4 landed; COFF-5 holds one slot; FRAMEWORK promoted into the other for FW-15
  (the L2→L3 wire — prerequisite for CPDF-10's reading_refs acceptance, and the
  highest-leverage independent item on the board). CPDF-10 additionally waits on
  DEC-35 (the OCR service vendor/account — Bob's; provisional: it stays queued).
- **The case-making run (REC-10 → REC-19 → REC-11 → …, the 2026-08-01 handover order)
  stays QUEUED under a now-DORMANT RECORD**, first in line when a slot frees or Bob
  reopens the thread. Its activation order inside RECORD is unchanged; DEC-8's
  no-act-surface-before-REC-19 doctrine stands.

**Measurement-only and test-estate items hold no slot** (`ORCHESTRATION.md`): COFF-6
(the office-corpus measurement — sets COFF-2's bound, runs IMMEDIATELY), CPDF-9 (the
OCR measurement — gates CPDF-10's whole design, run early) and REC-27/M0-lane work
run out of band whenever CONDUCT has integration capacity between area items.

## BOB INBOX — append-only. BOB writes here; CONDUCT drains it.

The producer/consumer split that makes an architectural change landable WITHOUT
pausing CONDUCT (`ORCHESTRATION.md`). BOB appends; CONDUCT is the sole writer of
everything below this section and drains the inbox as part of its loop, deleting an
entry only once it has been enacted below. The two parties write to disjoint regions,
so neither has to stop for the other.

_(drained by CONDUCT 2026-08-07 — **`IS-BUILD-PLAN.md` IS THE AUTHORITY for the IS build and SUPERSEDES the interim IS-1..IS-9 sequencing CONDUCT drained earlier the same day.** Enacted below.

**SUPERSESSION IS NEVER SILENT, so every interim item keeps its id, takes status `superseded`, and NAMES what replaced it** (`ORCHESTRATION.md` rule 3): IS-1 → PL-1 · IS-2 → PL-2 · IS-3 → PL-13 · IS-4 → PL-3 · IS-5 → PL-11 · IS-7 → PL-14 · IS-8 → PL-16 · IS-9 → FL-3. **IS-6 is NOT superseded — it LANDED**, and the plan's PL-5 row is satisfied by it. Likewise **REC-59 satisfies PL-6** and **REC-60 satisfies PL-7** (both marked done here; the plan confirmed REC-59 before scheduling and was written before REC-60 landed). REC-61 → PL-10 and REC-62 → PL-8 + PL-9, the plan splitting D-222's staging finer than the interim item did.

**THE PLAN IS NOT COPIED INTO THIS FILE, and that is a deliberate call.** `ORCHESTRATION.md`: *"A notification, not a second copy. Restating the content creates a copy that immediately starts rotting."* Forty item scopes transcribed here would be a second authority drifting from the first — the mirror-and-drift class this project refuses everywhere else, and the same reasoning that made REC-55 decline to publish a second copy of a predicate. **So the plan holds the SCOPE and this file holds what is RUNNABLE and its STATUS**, one pointer row per wave slot. *"Nothing is work until it is in `QUEUE.md`"* is satisfied by the pointer rows, not by transcription.

**WAVE POSITION AT DRAIN: W0 and W1 ARE ALREADY SATISFIED.** W1 slot A was PL-7 (REC-60, landed) and slot B was PL-5 (IS-6, landed) — so the two items CONDUCT ran before this entry arrived closed the plan's first working wave. **Current wave is W2: slot A = PL-8 (D-222 option A, discharging D-223), slot B = PL-1 (basis versions).** W0's lanes hold no slot and are queued beside them (FL-1's D-218 probe, the D-216 model check, VF-2's DEC-49 guard, SK-1's authoring) — and two of them, D-216 and D-218, are PRECONDITIONS the plan front-loads deliberately, because each reshapes an item BEFORE its wave.

**THE ONE FLAGGED FOLLOW-UP is recorded rather than left in the entry:** the post-processing task scope that produces live machine connections for UI-44 has **no item**. Raise it when W5 approaches, or UI-44 ships fixture-verified. Written onto the W-tracking row below so it is met at the right wave rather than remembered.

Fences, placements and controls from the earlier handover STAND — the plan corrected the sequencing, not the doctrine. No entries outstanding.)_

**2026-08-10 · BOB · THE DECIDED INDEX IS IN THE LOOP, AND THERE IS A NEW GATE.**
Branch `bob-corpus-index` (596c697), green, unmerged. What changed for CONDUCT:

- **`node tools/decided.mjs "<subject>"` before raising a question or writing a decision
  item.** Added to `CLAUDE.md`, so every session loads it. It answers from
  `docs/DECIDED.md`, generated from every ruling in the corpus — 585 of them, 161 KB
  against the corpus's 7.4 MB. Measured cause: the reading a kickoff demands is ~565k
  tokens, which no session can read, and only 12% of rulings are in `DECISIONS.md`.
- **`plancheck` now FAILS on a stale `docs/DECIDED.md`.** Any turn that rules on anything
  must run `node tools/decided.mjs` and commit the result, exactly as a version bump must
  leave `check-versions` green. This is a NEW failure mode in a gate CONDUCT runs before
  every push — the first stale-index failure will look like an unrelated break otherwise.
- **No queue item is superseded and no worker should be stopped.** Nothing else moved:
  the archive and ledger-rolling moves in `docs/development/CORPUS-STUDY.md` are
  SPECIFIED AND NOT EXECUTED, and one of them is gated on a hazard CONDUCT should know
  about before touching prose in bulk — **`mintid` derives its id floors by reading ids
  MENTIONED in prose, and its ledger is not committed, so removing prose that names a
  high id LOWERS the floor and a fresh clone re-issues an id already in use.**

An entry names: what changed, which queue items it affects, and whether any in-flight
work is superseded. It does NOT decide worker lifecycle — stopping a running worker is
CONDUCT's call.

Item format:

    ### <ID> · <queued | active | done | blocked | superseded>
    milestone:        <M0 … M10>
    scope:            <bounded description of the one piece>
    behind-interface: <I1 … I6 | none — what makes it independent>
    depends-on:       <IDs, or none>
    accepts-when:     <a command that passes, plus the negative control that must fail>
    added:            <date · CONDUCT|BOB>
    landed:           <commit, when done>

Standing gate for every item, from `VERIFICATION.md`: `npm run test:battery` green
(every suite, all reported), the item's own `accepts-when:`, the negative control RUN
and recorded in the suite's `NEGATIVE CONTROL:` line, and `npm run test:coverage`
showing no new unreached op.

## M0 — VERIFICATION · cross-cutting, a BACKGROUND LANE (holds no slot)

Test-estate work spanning every area. CONDUCT spawns a worker per item with a claim on
the specific files. These are cheap, they touch no plane behaviour, and they raise the
floor everything else is judged against.

### M0-10 · done
milestone: M0
scope: **`migrate.test.mjs` fails under concurrency and passes alone — raised by UI-39 rather than smoothed, which is the only reason it is visible.** Observed while the battery ran alongside a coverage run and negative-control runs; green when run by itself. **A suite whose result depends on what else is running is not a suite** — it will eventually fail in someone's integration run and be dismissed as flake, and the first time it is dismissed correctly is indistinguishable from the first time it is dismissed wrongly. **Establish the CAUSE rather than serialising the runner**, which would hide it: shared temp paths, a fixed port, a Miniflare sandbox name, a shared file, or an assumption about wall-clock ordering are the candidates. If the honest fix genuinely is serialisation, say WHY at the site and make the reason survive — a suite pinned to serial for an unrecorded reason is debt nobody can pay off. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** this is a test-estate item, so the question is whether any OTHER suite shares the resource this one contends on. The battery's own temp accounting already reports sandbox and orphan counts — use it as an instrument rather than adding a new one.
behind-interface: none — test estate, no plane behaviour
depends-on: none
accepts-when: the cause NAMED and recorded at the site, not merely worked around; `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green **run concurrently with `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict`**, repeated enough times to be evidence rather than one lucky pass, and the number of repetitions STATED; `$?` read unpiped on both. NEGATIVE CONTROL run and recorded — reintroduce the contention and the failure returns, so the fix is shown to be the fix rather than a timing change that moved it.
added: 2026-08-05 · CONDUCT (UI-39's delegation; test estate, holds no slot)
landed: 2026-08-08 (worker `agent-a0498732e9b265446`, branch `worktree-agent-a0498732e9b265446` — NOT pushed; CONDUCT integrates). **THE CAUSE IS A HARDCODED SHARED FILESYSTEM GROUND, AND IT IS NOT A CONCURRENCY DEFECT IN THE SUBJECT — the brief's suspect list was right about the CLASS and the row's own framing was the thing to check.** `migrate.test.mjs` rooted its whole fixture tree at the literal `/tmp/civicos-fixture`, **outside the `$TMPDIR` `sandbox.mjs` owns**, and WIPED it at entry and exit. Two concurrent copies of THE SAME SUITE therefore destroyed each other: one died `ENOTEMPTY` when its opening `rmSync` raced the other's writes; the other's `discoverBundles` ran after the other's wipe, found **2 of 3 bundles** and dereferenced `undefined`. **Both modes KILL the suite rather than failing an assertion, so the battery reports NO TALLY — D-93's shape**, which is why this read as "flaky" rather than as a failure naming a cause. **PROVED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, and the re-arm sharpened the diagnosis rather than confirming it: restoring ONLY the shared literal came back GREEN 3 of 3.** The cause is the WIPE of a shared path, not the sharing — which is why the probes that merely READ `/tmp/corpus` and `/tmp/tok` are not instances. Restoring the wipe beside the literal reproduced **3 of 3 red**. **THE FLAKE WAS THE CHEAP HALF HERE TOO.** `hygiene.test.mjs` required every temp-making suite to IMPORT `sandbox.mjs`; both offenders did, and both passed, because the check certified the IMPORT and never the CONTAINMENT. And `battery.mjs` counts residue only INSIDE `$TMPDIR`, so a suite writing outside it is not under-reported but **unreported** — the battery printed `0 directories, 0 miniflare sandboxes` while **12 MB of Durable Object and R2 SQLite sat in `/tmp/mfp` accumulating since 2026-07-31**, from `bootstrap.test.mjs`, a SECOND instance nobody had noticed. Wrong in the generous direction. Raised as **D-235**, with D-186's disposition corrected in the same turn. **CLASS CLOSED STRUCTURALLY, not patched twice:** no suite may root a filesystem ground at an absolute-path LITERAL, the principle being that a safe ground is DERIVED at runtime rather than fixed in source, so no allowlist can go stale (REC-70's lesson). **SWEEP: exactly TWO instances in the battery, both fixed; THREE sites are the class RECOGNISED AND NOT DEFECTS** — `migrate/local-plane.mjs` (`/tmp/plane-persist` and a fixed port 8787: a hand-run dev plane whose persistence is the point, discovered by no runner) and the three probes, which READ operator-supplied paths and never write or wipe them. **WHAT THE CHECK CANNOT SEE IS STATED AT THE SITE:** a literal built by concatenation or held in a variable, a path from an env var, and a shared NON-filesystem resource — **a fixed PORT is the same class and this check is blind to it.** **D-186 UNDER GENUINE CONCURRENCY IS SETTLED — the question M0-13 named and could not answer.** Per-process `$TMPDIR` ownership **HOLDS**: 12 truly overlapping processes, distinct sandbox each, `$TMPDIR` redirected in each, every `mkdtemp` nested inside its own, every marker surviving all siblings, no foreign pid's file in any sandbox, 0 of 12 left after exit. The probe is ARMED — sharing one sandbox reports DOES NOT HOLD on four axes, because the first sibling to exit `rmSync`s the ground out from under the rest. **ACCEPTANCE: FIVE consecutive rounds, each running the battery, `coverage.mjs --strict` AND a contention loop hammering `migrate`+`bootstrap` SIMULTANEOUSLY** — because for this item a repetition count alone is not evidence: the historical failure needed a second copy of the contending suite in flight, and sequential batteries never produce one. All five CLEAN, per-suite counts identical: battery exit 0 at **122/123 green + 1 named skip (`pdf-worker`, D-232 — not this item's), 7,662 assertions**, coverage exit 0, and **3,652 concurrent contending suite runs across the five rounds with 0 non-zero exits**. Baseline MEASURED TWICE on the untouched tree (7,659, the two runs agreeing on EVERY suite) and the delta attributed by re-running it: **the ONLY suite that moved is `hygiene.test.mjs` 485 → 488**, my three new assertions; `migrate` held 40 and `bootstrap` held 18, so neither fix touched an assertion. `--strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; register 395 → 398 arms, fullest 21 → 24. UI harness from the repo root, exit 0. **8 control arms, and THREE came back wrong — all three about the INSTRUMENT, all recorded rather than smoothed:** the first re-arm went GREEN because it restored only half the cause (which is what sharpened the diagnosis); the concurrency harness labelled four clean `bootstrap` runs "suite died" because its tally regex demanded a name prefix `bootstrap` does not print; and the arm driver's output filter was case-SENSITIVE, so it showed only `FAIL` lines and once hid a crash banner entirely. **A fourth was my own error caught by the instrument being right:** the register did not move for my new arms, which looked exactly like D-233 — the real cause was that my declaration never used the documented `-> ` grammar. Rewritten; the register then moved by exactly 3. **The over-strictness arm also exposed a real weakness in my own check** — its unbounded findings list made the suite fail without printing a tally, D-93's mode inside a check written against a different one; the pattern no longer spans newlines and the message is capped.

milestone: M0
scope: **Stop the battery leaking temp directories (D-186).** Three parts: (a) the
  miniflare harness disposes of its sandbox in a `finally`, so a THROWING suite still
  cleans up — the leak is worst exactly when tests fail; (b) each suite that mints its
  own sandbox disposes of it the same way; (c) `scripts/battery.mjs` sweeps ORPHANS at
  start — older than the current run only, never a blanket wipe, because a concurrent
  worker's live sandbox must survive (that is not hypothetical: it was live during the
  cleanup that found this). The OCR probes' npm-installs into temp are bounded
  separately and may stay, since they are probes and not battery.
behind-interface: none — test estate
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green twice in a row with the
  `$TMPDIR` `miniflare-*` count NOT growing between runs (that comparison IS the
  assertion); a suite made to throw mid-run still leaves no sandbox behind; negative
  control — remove the `finally` disposal and the count grows again by the number of
  miniflare instances, which is the state today.
added: 2026-08-04 · BOB
landed: (merge on main, worker 7b39d33) — **THE ITEM'S PREMISE WAS INVERTED AND THE WORKER MEASURED IT**: the leak is worst on SUCCESS, not failure. Measured 3 modes x 3 runs — succeed-and-exit leaked 3/3, throw-mid-run leaked 0/3. Miniflare#dispose() disarms its own exit hook and then calls removeDir FIRE-AND-FORGET without awaiting; every suite ends `await dispose(); process.exit()`, which hygiene REQUIRES so a lingering handle cannot turn green into a hang — so process.exit beats the unawaited removal with the safety net already down, while a THROWING suite never reaches dispose() and miniflare's still-armed hook cleans up perfectly. **A `finally` would have fixed nothing.** 23,263 directories was the record of a battery working correctly, scaling with GREEN integration throughput. THE FIX: test/sandbox.mjs owns $TMPDIR for the process and rmSyncs it in process.on('exit') + SIGINT/TERM/HUP — SYNCHRONOUS is the point, exit listeners run to completion so they cannot be outrun; part (b) falls out structurally (every mkdtemp lands inside the owned dir); 84 suites take one import line; hygiene DERIVES the requirement from source so the list cannot fall behind the directory (D-93's failure mode), which matters because ~80 test:<suite> scripts run outside the battery. THE SWEEP, three rules because the two kinds carry different evidence: bio-battery-<pid>-* names its owner so kill(pid,0) decides with NO CLOCK (EPERM reads as ALIVE — the one error that must); legacy prefixes by age at runStart−2h GRACE (plain older-than-this-run is unsafe: a battery that began 60s earlier has older directories and is still using them); and over both, anything a live workerd/node still holds a descriptor in is spared outright — NOT THEORETICAL: a suite from another live worktree was found running at 1h28m and survived all seven sweeps, verified by name. THE ASSERTION MADE ATTRIBUTABLE: the first version went RED ON A GREEN RUN (a foreign un-fixed battery writing into the same $TMPDIR) — a false red on the battery is worse than the leak it reports — so the runner mkdtemps its own sandbox and spawns every suite with TMPDIR pointing at it; residue there is THIS run's leak exactly, while the host count is still reported and attributed. Battery green TWICE (95/95, 5172; 0 directories both runs); --strict 129/129. NCs RUN x2 + a driven throw (comment out the exit hook → the runner exits 1 naming 84 leaked sandboxes WHILE ALL 95 SUITES REPORT GREEN AND ALL 5172 ASSERTIONS PASS — precisely why this ran unnoticed for weeks, and why the instrument belongs in the runner not a suite; strip an import → hygiene names the file). D-186 CLOSED.

milestone: M8
scope: **The refuse-weight gate cannot see a query selection that moved — routed out of UI-25 as a DELEGATION, and CONDUCT decided the mechanism rather than raising it.** MEASURED by UI-25 against `store.mjs`: `selectionResolve` computes `stopped = moved && weight === "refuse"`, and `moved = revised.length + removed + added > 0` — **`digestChanged` is not a term in it.** So a `kind:"query"` selection whose membership swaps AT A CONSTANT COUNT reports `moved:false`, and a refuse-weight act passes the gate silently over a set the operator did not see. `retire` and `release` are both refuse-weight and both take a selection handle. That is precisely "a state transition landing on a set the operator did not see", which `selectionResolve`'s OWN comment says the refuse weight exists to prevent. **Nothing is exposed today** — the finder offers only `cite`, which is report-weight — so this is LATENT, and latent is why it is worth fixing now rather than after a surface offers a refuse-weight act over a query lease. **THE FIX IS DECIDED, and the reasoning is recorded here rather than sent to Bob because it is a mechanism call, which is CONDUCT's:** do NOT fold `digestChanged` into `moved` — `moved` correctly means per-row movement and every enumerated caller depends on that meaning. Have the REFUSE GATE test `moved || digestChanged`. UI-25's recommendation, adopted; it changes one predicate at one site and leaves the published `moved` semantics that two surfaces read. **CORRECTED 2026-08-05 at integration: this scope originally said SIX and that figure was not reproducible against the tree.** REC-55 measured `app.html` reading a selection's `moved` at exactly **two** sites (`selectionMoved`, `FINDSEL.moved`); the other two `.moved` hits are an unrelated field on a different op. CONDUCT wrote the number without measuring it. The ruling is unaffected — not folding is still right — but a figure stated as though measured, and not measured, is the same failure as the ~4,900 line count that stood for weeks. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not this one predicate — it is every place the plane decides whether a selection is safe to act on, and whether any OTHER gate reads `moved` where it means "has this answer changed at all". Expect more than one; six items this week each found more than their brief predicted.

### REC-57 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **Two plane ops publish a bound they do not name, forcing every consumer to guess — the UI-39 class, one layer down (UI → RECORD delegation).** MEASURED by UI-39 against the plane: (a) **`op=readingname` caps at 100/500 and publishes neither a `limit` nor a truncation flag**, and its `count` is the length of what it SENT — indistinguishable from what EXISTS, which is precisely the ambiguity that makes an unstated bound read as completeness. (b) **`op=tasks` publishes a `counts` block but no `truncated`, while its sibling `op=queue` publishes exactly that flag** — so two ops on the same surface answer the same question in two different shapes, and a consumer that reads one correctly reads the other wrongly. UI-39 consumed what was there and could go no further honestly; this is the half that must move in the plane. **The consumer is a COMPLETENESS CLAIM in both cases**, which is why this is not cosmetic: UI-25's whole item existed because a member with more than 500 hits could cite only the first 500 into a case. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not these two ops — it is **every capped op, and whether it publishes what its cap was and whether it truncated.** Read the roster of capped ops off `store.mjs`/`query.mjs` the way UI-39's walk does rather than listing them by hand, and where an op already publishes the fact, say so rather than adding a second spelling of it. Any shape change goes through the `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md` protocol — these are I3 ops with real consumers.

### IS-2 · superseded
milestone: M9
scope: **The STATE MACHINE over versions — and it is to be stated as THE SIXTH MACHINE, in those words.** Four states, **reversible**; every transition a MEMBER act with the four beats; reasons required on rejection, deferral and dismissal; **machine identity refused on each**; proposals aggregate and age (§6.4); publishes through `op=affordances`. **THE NEGATIVE CONTROL WAS CORRECTED 2026-08-07 BECAUSE THE FIRST DRAFT WOULD HAVE PASSED VACUOUSLY, and that correction is the most important sentence in this item.** `VERIFICATION.md` rule 3a: a rule enforced in N places carries an assertion at EACH place — and **an `ai` credential refused at the CREDENTIAL layer absorbs the control before the transition refusal ever runs**, so a naive control proves nothing about this layer. The fence lives in **three layers — credential scope (IS-5), endpoint (IS-4), transition (IS-2)** — and the control must **break EACH layer with the others HELD OPEN, requiring THAT layer's own assertion to fail.** Acceptance also carries §14b.4: **every fence is CODE, never a line in a skill or a prompt.** Depends additionally on **D-78's `surfaced_by` fix being real** — verify that rather than assuming it.
behind-interface: I5
depends-on: IS-1

NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded — **(1) THE THREE-LAYER ARM, and it is the item: break the credential layer with endpoint and transition HELD OPEN and require the credential assertion to fail; then the endpoint with the other two open; then the transition with the other two open. A control that breaks all three at once proves nothing, which is exactly what the first draft did. **AND IT HAS NOW HAPPENED FOR REAL, A LAYER EARLY — treat this as EVIDENCE rather than as a warning: IS-6's C-22.4 control left its suite GREEN at 98/98, because the condition-vocabulary rule had TWO IMPLEMENTATIONS — one inlined inside a second function — so removing either left the other ABSORBING the control, and neither implementation had a control that proved anything about it.** IS-6 fixed it structurally (one implementation, reached through two doors, with an arm pinning that there is only one). Your three layers are three chances for exactly that, so **pin the COUNT OF IMPLEMENTATIONS as well as breaking each layer**; (2) drop a reason requirement and it FAILS naming the transition; (3) move a fence into a prompt and the source assertion FAILS; (4) polarity checked on every pin.

### REC-63 · done
milestone: M2
scope: **DEC-56's enactment — the record CARRIES the doubt at `verified` rather than un-saying a verification.** Bob ruled the principle 2026-08-06 across DEC-56/57/58 together: **ACT, AND SAY WHAT YOU COULD NOT ESTABLISH.** Applied here it settles the shape CONDUCT recommended and Bob's principle independently reaches: **not a `verified -> collected` retraction edge, and not silence** — a standing marker at `verified` stating that the route cannot be shown. **The reasoning, so it is not re-opened:** retracting a verification restates a group's own past act, and this project's posture is that correction moves FORWARD (DEC-19); a marker also matches what is actually true — the bytes may be fine and the ROUTE is what we cannot show, which is a statement about our evidence rather than about the document. **The state and the finding will DISAGREE ON PURPOSE** — a document sits at `verified` while `op=audit` reports it — **and that has to be legible rather than look like a bug**, which is this item's hardest part and its acceptance. REC-54 already built the derivation and the refusal (`EVIDENCE_INSUFFICIENT`, stated and never guessed), so this is the marker and its surfacing, not the detection. **Closes the honest route D-200 named and REC-56 found has no act behind it** (D-204): a bundle whose chain cannot be reconstructed currently has nowhere to go but `retired`, which asserts something quite different. **Every refusal this adds carries a C-number, a DEC-49 error code and a canned translation** — that ruling landed the same day and is not optional.
behind-interface: I5
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline and trust it over this brief** (main is 105/105 at 5,969 as written) — any delta ATTRIBUTED per suite; with a document whose chain cannot be reconstructed carrying a STATED marker at `verified`, the audit reporting it, and **the disagreement between state and finding legible in both** rather than readable as a defect; no `verified -> collected` edge added; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded — (1) **add the retraction edge and the suite FAILS naming DEC-19's correction-moves-forward posture** (the shape Bob's principle rejected must be pinned out, not merely not-built); (2) let the marker be silent — carried in state but not stated to a reader — and it FAILS, because silence is the exact failure mode the ruling names; (3) a document with a GOOD chain must carry no marker, so the two cannot collapse the other way; (4) polarity checked.
added: 2026-08-07 · CONDUCT (enacting DEC-56)

### REC-64 · done
milestone: M7
scope: **DEC-49's enactment — every refusable condition carries an ERROR CODE with a CANNED TRANSLATION, and an untranslated code FAILS THE HARNESS.** Bob, 2026-08-06: *"I have no problem with those messages being translated... These conditions should include an error code that there's a canned translation for."* **IT AMENDS DEC-8**: a surface may now render an AUTHORED translation keyed on a code the plane sent — it still may NOT COMPUTE a refusal, **the code must be RECEIVED, never inferred**, so DEC-8's actual protection is intact and only its wording changes (DEC-8's entry now carries the amendment note). **THE GUARD IS WHAT MAKES THIS SAFE AND IS NOT OPTIONAL: every code a surface can receive has a translation, and an untranslated code FAILS THE HARNESS rather than reaching a member.** Without it this degrades into thirteen surfaces each inventing wording — the drift REC-43 closed on the co-attestation fence. **Build-time or runtime lookup is an implementation choice Bob explicitly left open**, so choose it and say why at the site. **The subject is SMALLER than it looks and two findings resize it:** the published-case-page half is handled by DEC-59 (definitions become a case ELEMENT and a PDF section, with the effective bias), and **`sha256` and the file paths want DEFINING, not rewording — a reworded address is a broken one.** What remains is the GATE, and there the jargon is the small part: three of the four sentences at `store.mjs:12362` explain the SYSTEM'S DESIGN — that only a salted derivation is stored, and that the refusal deliberately will not say which of two causes applied so it cannot enumerate which roles hold credentials. **Both are true and the second is a real security property — keep them; a person who cannot get in did not ask for either.** **This is a PRECONDITION-shaped item for the whole IS set**, since every fence IS adds owes a code and a translation. **RIDER, measured at PL-12's integration and it RESIZES this item: 32 of the plane's existing refusals are ALREADY FAILING the DEC-49 guard on `main`, and PL-12 measured that rather than inferring it** — it checked out the parent commit with its own files removed, ran the guard, and got **exit 1 with 32 failures, the same 32 and the same offending-code set as on its own branch, diffed rather than eyeballed.** **The cause is SCOPE, not a defect anyone introduced:** PL-1's rows carry `where: 'src/store.mjs promote'`, which makes **the whole 939-line `promote` a governed site**, so `NO_BODY`, `EXISTS`, `CAS_STALE` and a dozen long-standing refusals are now measured. **Two shapes, and choosing between them is this item's first decision:** give every `promote` refusal a row — REC-64 arriving early, in the largest function in the plane — **or let a `where` name a narrower site than a whole function**, which is the smaller change and may be the honest one, since a governed *site* and a governed *function* are different claims. **RIDER from SK-1, 2026-08-08, and it is this item's SIZE stated in one number: THE DOCTRINE PACK CAN RENDER ONE MACHINE FENCE OF TWELVE.** Eleven of the twelve `MACHINE_CANNOT_*` codes carry no canned translation, so the pack that exists to tell an agent what it may not do can state one twelfth of it IN WORDS. **Read it beside D-229, which measured the SAME TWELVE from the opposite direction — that eleven of them are not doing the work at all, an unrelated payload guard is.** Two items arriving independently at one set: the fences neither FIRE as fences nor can be EXPLAINED. **So the eleven are the first eleven translations this item writes**, and the pack is a consumer that proves each one landed — a translation with no renderer is the hand-copy shape this item already forbids. VF-2 sized the whole (74 in reach, 287 out); SK-1 names which eleven come first and why. **RIDER from UI-38: §14a says the running-session surface must SAY SO when the capability is unavailable, and that sentence is member-facing refusal wording — UI-38 correctly LEFT IT rather than authoring it.** It is owed a code and a canned translation here, like every other refusable condition, rather than being written at the surface.
behind-interface: I3
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — measure your own baseline first — any delta attributed per suite; **every refusable condition carrying a code, every code a surface can receive carrying a translation, and the harness FAILING on an untranslated code**, with the code-to-translation map read from ONE place rather than copied (a hand copy agrees at zero cost — measured five times); `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded — (1) **add a refusable condition with no translation and the harness FAILS naming it** — this is the guard the ruling calls not optional; (2) let a surface COMPUTE a refusal rather than receive its code and it FAILS naming DEC-8, whose protection this must leave intact; (3) neuter the walk that enumerates refusable conditions and its REACH fails as a DELTA with the corpus size printed; (4) an over-strictness arm — a correctly coded-and-translated refusal phrased unlike anything you wrote must PASS.
added: 2026-08-07 · CONDUCT (enacting DEC-49; amends DEC-8)

### REC-79 · done (landed 2026-08-09, worktree agent-a0bea725408eb06f8)
milestone: M7
interface: I3 (a refusal gains fields; no op contract is reshaped — the change is ADDITIVE and IC-REC-79 registers it)
depends-on: REC-64 (landed — its measured residual is this item's subject), REC-78 (landed — its two unreachable codes are DECLARED here with their receipt, never re-derived)
scope: **REC-64'S REMAINING SWEEP MADE TRACTABLE, AND ONE FAMILY PROVED END TO END. It does NOT try to translate the remainder and says so.** REC-64 landed DEC-49's enactment partly and stated plainly that it could not close the class; that honesty is why this item exists.
**THE HEADLINE IS A CORRECTION TO THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN SENTENCE.** The census NOTE read *"N of M refusal codes have NO canned translation AND ARE NOT IN REACH of a surface today"* — **41 of the 248 WERE in reach**, and they are the exact set arm B names by code three lines above it on every run. One number carried two claims and the second was false for a sixth of it. Nobody was careless: the two claims were true together the day it was written and drifted apart. **A record claiming more than it can support, inside the instrument whose job is to stop exactly that.**
**THE PARTITION (new arm F), ordered BY THE DECISION EACH CODE NEEDS, summing exactly and gated at zero — because the count was conflating four different defects:** F1 UNREACHABLE, needs a DECISION not a sentence (**2** — REC-78's, declared with its receipt and labelled as declared); F2 CONSTRUCTED, not a code at all (**1** — `SIG_`, a concatenation prefix the census records as though it were a code while the real `SIG_*` codes are invisible); F3 SUBSYSTEM, needs a determination whether it crosses the wire (**18**); F4 MULTI-SITE, needs a STRUCTURAL change (**93** — a row holds one `where` and a code minted at fifteen sites cannot honestly name one); F5 IN REACH, one site, **needs a sentence NOW (6)**; F6 out of reach, one site, needs a sentence when its surface exists (**126**). **THE ACTIONABLE SLICE IS SIX CODES, not 248** — `BAD_LOCATOR, GATE_REFUSED, NOT_AN_OWNER, NOT_CITABLE, NO_ATTESTATION, NO_SUCH_CAPTURE` — and the next item is a slice rather than a swamp.
**THE WORKED FAMILY IS THE ADMISSION GATE (C-38, `ADMISSION_CHECKS`), and FOUR OF ITS SIX REFUSALS CARRIED NO CODE AT ALL.** `"unauthenticated"`, `"forbidden for token class"`, `"this operation requires a machine credential"` and the scope refusal answered with a bare `error:` sentence and nothing a surface could key on — invisible to DEC-49's guard AND absent from its census, **because a census of CODES cannot count a refusal that has none.** The gate every single caller passes through was outside the rule governing everything behind it.
**IT WAS FOUND BY TRYING TO GOVERN THE SITE, NOT BY READING IT** — which is the reusable half. A region placed there reported nothing to judge, because arm C's outcome reader could not see `return json({ … }, 403)`, **the control plane's universal refusal spelling, 77 of them in `index.mjs` alone.** Nothing had ever governed a control-plane refusal. The fix INVERTS rather than naming `json`: a call in return position is a TRANSPORT, so its FIRST argument is the outcome — true for `Response.json`, for `new Response`, and for the wrapper nobody has written yet.
**AND THE LIVE DEFECT IT CLOSES (PL-18's):** `app.html` hand-authored *"Capturing needs a member holding contribute"* for `NOT_CAPABLE` — a **plane-wide** refusal minted for whatever capability the op needed, so a member refused for `create_projects` was told about contributing. The surface now renders the plane's canned translation verbatim, GENERALLY rather than as a special case, so the next translated code needs no edit there.
**WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO, stated rather than left to be discovered:** it did not close F4's 93 multi-site codes, and it met that class INSIDE its own worked family — `NOT_CAPABLE` is minted at two sites and C-38.5's `where` names only one. What is closed is that **both sites read the ONE row, so they cannot drift into two wordings**, and a suite drives both through the op and asserts the sentences are byte-identical. The `where` limitation is written at the site, not only here.

D-218's reasoning is CONFIRMED — and that is NOT the finding that matters.** **50.0 seconds of wall time waiting on subrequests cost 4.29 ms of billed CPU** (~0.16 ms per awaited subrequest, independent of wait length): **waiting is effectively free.** A run-shaped loop over REAL plane payloads costs 24.29 ms at 25 turns and 4,488.9 ms at 400, growing ~n^1.9 **because the transcript is re-serialised every turn** — extrapolating to ~1,100 turns inside the 30 s default. **BUT MEMORY BINDS FIRST, BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE: at 200 turns the isolate is at 120.4 MB of 128 MB while CPU is still 2.5% of its ceiling — so a segment sized on CPU HEADROOM WOULD BE ~10x TOO LONG.** 100-150 turns per segment sits inside both, and **the single change that lifts both curves is to stop re-serialising the whole transcript per turn** — which is FL-3's shape, sized before FL-3 is written, which is exactly why the plan front-loads this lane. **THE NEGATIVE CONTROL EARNED ITS EXISTENCE:** every figure passes a gate that THROWS unless provenance is the platform's billing surface, and the deployed self-timed arm **WOULD HAVE LIED — its own clock read 0 ms where the platform billed 498.57 ms for the same work.** The gate's empty case is guarded too: seven bad shapes including `undefined`, `null`, `{}` and `vendor-docs` all refused while a real reading is still accepted. **D-190's posture rides the same row and is stated with its limit:** `dbBytes` gives 176,657 B/bundle → ~60,800 bundles against the vendor's 10 GB claim (**denominator theirs, labelled as theirs**) — but **the measured point has an EMPTY meaning layer**, so the slope prices capture only and is **a floor on cost and an overstatement of capacity**; D-224 is what moves it. **The platform carries NO per-object storage surface at all** — measured, so nobody looks for one again. and control (2b) proves the TOTALITY is what does the work rather than a forbidden-key list, because a third key called `headline` that no list predicted still fires. **UNGRADED LEGS ARE INERT AND NAMED AS TWO FACTS, broken one at a time: (7a) leaves every arithmetic arm GREEN at 86/4 while the record stops saying what it is standing on** — which is exactly why naming needs its own control — and (7b) fails the other way at 76/14. **OWED CONTROL 3 RUN: strip DEC-40's filter line and C-29.8 fires and NOTHING LEAVES AT ALL** — no pair, no letters. **So (1b) took BOTH defences down to name the consequence: the answer LEAVES, carrying a real pair over a reading nobody adopted with no line saying so — a what-if indistinguishable from the record's own answer.** One defence down proves teeth and says nothing about harm; Asserting the literal would have shipped *we could not finish looking* for *nothing was established* — so PL-14 asserts the BEHAVIOUR in both states, building the `undetermined` arm by **driving a nine-deep inquiry chain past `QUEUE_ANCESTOR_DEPTH`** rather than typing a state name, and raised the documents rather than editing them. **AT INTEGRATION, A C-NUMBER FAMILY COLLISION AND A TRAP THIS PROJECT HAS NOW PAID FOR TWICE: PL-11 and PL-14 EACH MEASURED C-29 AS FREE and each allocated nine numbers in it.** Both were right when they looked. - **REC-73** — **THE FENCES PROVED, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS TEN TIMES LARGER THAN THE DEBT ROW PREDICTED. **AND CONDUCT RAISED D-231 AT THIS INTEGRATION, from something it saw itself rather than from a report: `suggest.test.mjs` IS INTERMITTENTLY RED UNDER THE BATTERY AND GREEN STANDALONE.** PL-14 saw it once from a tree carrying none of its files; CONDUCT saw **59/2 under the battery**, then 61/0 on three consecutive standalone runs and a clean full battery, on a different tree. - **M0-13** — **D-231 DIAGNOSED, AND THE FLAKE WAS THE CHEAP HALF. Landed 2026-08-08. THE CAUSE IS A WALL CLOCK INSIDE A COMPARISON THAT HAD TO BE TIME-FREE — a live PLANE defect, not a test defect.** `suggestVersion`'s local `substanceOf` stripped `name` and `derived_from` from PL-1's canonical composition before comparing a candidate against every held reading — **but a composition's ground rows carry an `at`, and `#suggestionFrontmatter` stamps it with the server's clock at SECOND resolution.** The candidate is stamped now; every held reading was stamped when it was written. **So §6 rule 8's duplicate gate fired ONLY WHEN BOTH SUBMISSIONS LANDED INSIDE THE SAME ONE-SECOND BUCKET.** Driven through the control plane, one inquiry per scenario: **0 ms apart → refused; 100 ms → refused; 1,200 ms → LANDED; 2,500 ms → LANDED.** **THAT IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS: *a run adds its output as a new version only if it differs in substance from every existing one* was UNENFORCED against any retry loop that paused a second — which is every real one.** The intermittent redness was the symptom by which a live gate's absence became visible at all. **THE CAUSE IS PROVED RATHER THAN CORRELATED, and in both directions:** re-arming it (control D-231a) reproduces the historical signature **deterministically** at 61/2 with exactly the pair CONDUCT saw, while CHECK 3's immediate arms stay GREEN — which is the defect's own shape, works inside a second and not outside — and a boundary crossed only by the later submission reproduces **PL-14's 60/1**. Concurrency was only the machine load that made the suite slow enough to cross a second. **CLASS SWEEP: exactly ONE instance of *a server clock inside bytes compared against stored bytes* exists in `src/**`**, and the freeze, publish idempotence, manifest, bias-drift, selection-drift and CAS comparisons all carry no clock. **Two sites are the class RECOGNISED AND CLOSED ON PURPOSE** — `container.mjs` pins ZIP entries to the DOS epoch, and `monitor_fired` REMEMBERS its tick epoch *because a retry arrives with a new `Date.now()`* — so the sweep distinguishes a defect from a deliberate closure rather than counting both. **FIVE CONSECUTIVE BATTERIES, all exit 0, per-suite counts IDENTICAL across all five**, deliberately run while a foreign battery ran in another checkout — the exact concurrency that used to redden this suite, left alone rather than avoided. Delta +3 (`suggest` 61→63, `planning-hygiene` 266→267), attributed by diffing per-suite counts against a re-run baseline; `--strict` exit 0, unchanged; UI harness exit 0. **12 control arms, 0 behaved other than declared, and TWO CAME BACK WRONG FIRST:** its over-strictness arm failed because the version name it chose carried a COMMA, which C-25.2's grammar forbids — failing via `VERSION_NAME_NOT_UNIQUE` from `promote`, nothing to do with its subject; **and it predicted the control register would move 388→390 for two new arms and it DID NOT MOVE AT ALL — which is how D-233 was found.** **ON D-186 IT REFUSES TO OVERCLAIM, and the refusal is the useful part:** the battery is sequential and the mechanism intra-process, so `sandbox.mjs` was never in play; it reports the printed figures (0 directories, 0 sandboxes left; host orphans 44 → 44) and states plainly that **an unchanged count shows the sweep spares a foreign owner as designed and is NOT evidence that per-process `$TMPDIR` isolation holds under genuine concurrency, which nothing here measured.** **TWO RESIDUALS RAISED: D-234** — the SAME gate is also defeated by a QUOTATION MARK, with no clock and no flakiness, because the candidate is composed from RAW ARGS while the compared bytes went through `#fmSafe`; measured all inside one second so the clock cannot be the cause, and not fixed because the honest fix changes what the endpoint PUBLISHES. **And D-233** — the control register's arms tally is blind to a declaration written in the numbered form: `suggest.test.mjs` declares 12 arms, runs all 12, and scores ZERO; four of 119 suites score zero and "388" is an unflagged undercount. **A decision is recorded for Bob: a test-estate lane item opened `src/store.mjs` on purpose**, with the alternative, the recommendation and a reversal cost that does NOT rise as data accumulates — which is why it shipped provisionally rather than stopping.
**AT INTEGRATION, A DEBT-NUMBER COLLISION OF THE SAME SHAPE AS THE C-29/C-30 ONE, one item later: M0-13 and CONDUCT each took D-232 within the hour** — M0-13 for the quotation-mark defect, CONDUCT for `pdf-worker`'s dark suite at FL-2's integration — **and neither could see the other.** CONDUCT's was pushed first so its number stands; **M0-13's moved to D-234**, recorded at the row rather than silently swapped, because the worker's report, claim and suite header all name D-232. **Its D-232 row had also been written without a line break and merged as a continuation of D-231's row** — split at integration. **Verified after both: battery 121 suites / 120 green + 1 named skip at 7,379, run FOUR consecutive times with identical counts; `--strict` exit 0; UI harness exit 0.**

**POSITION AT 2026-08-08 (superseding the line below, which is kept for its wave history): PUSHED GREEN at `c31d875` — battery 119/119 at 7,285, `--strict` exit 0 (158 ops, 155 checks, all named), UI harness exit 0, plancheck clean. W0's SLOT-FREE LANES ARE FULLY DRAINED — FL-1, the D-216 model check, VF-2 and SK-1 have all landed — AND SO IS W5: slot A ran PL-4 then PL-11, slot B ran PL-14.** - **FL-3** — **THE RUN HARNESS (IS-9) — landed 2026-08-08, W7 slot A. THE CONTROL FLOW IS DATA, AND THE DRIVER HOLDS NO DECISION.** `CONTROL_FLOW` is eleven rows, each declaring `does` / `judged` / `logs` / `to`; `nextStep` is held to the edges its row declares; the module is **PURE — no fetch, no clock, no globals, all asserted.** Part A walks the table exhaustively over **6,912 states**; Part B drives the SAME table through `POST /run` inside workerd over a real service binding, so the table is not merely well-formed, it is the thing that runs. **Every plan-row requirement maps to a NAMED PLACE rather than to a claim: dedup-before-write is the ABSENT EDGE from `compose` to `submit`** (a rule enforced by a missing transition rather than by a check), **F10 is the `submit --refused--> adjust` ROW with `adjustedFrom` as its enforced precondition**, four-level fan-out is pinned against **the plane's own `OBSERVATION_LEVELS` by source read** rather than re-typed, and query-never-load consumes PL-9's op — **no second reader built.** **IS-9(d) DISCHARGED: `runtime-ceiling-reached` HAD THE WORD AND NOT THE PRODUCER**, which `airun.mjs` itself recorded and named IS-9 as the builder of. The driver spends one `runtime` unit per plane call and **the PLANE writes the condition at its own exit; the word is never sent by this member — asserted BEHAVIOURALLY, zero request bodies contain it.** **THE LARGEST CALL IN THE ITEM WAS ONE THE BRIEF DID NOT FLAG: FL-2's OWN FENCE BLOCKED FL-3's ACCEPTANCE.** `"it calls no mutating op"` was **TIGHTER THAN THE RULE IT ENFORCED** — the plan says *writes nothing DIRECTLY*, and PL-11's `ai` class is specified as writing *only PL-3's endpoint and PL-4's table*, **so FL-2's fence made PL-11's declared scope a scope with no consumer.** Corrected to an exact **pinned set of nine ops read from the plane's own `AI_RUN_ACTIONS`**, so the two cannot drift; the purer alternative (member returns a plan, plane writes) was rejected **on measurement rather than taste — it is unbuildable from here, because `RECORD` owns a calling side that does not exist.** **IC-33 SETTLED by CONDUCT, and the lesson is recorded at the IC: a fence tighter than its rule is not a safer fence — it is an undeclared interface change wearing the costume of caution, invisible until the item that needs the reach arrives one wave later.** **I8 STAYS PROVISIONAL at 0.1.0, and CONDUCT accepted FL-3's verdict in full rather than taking the bump it was offered.** The third reason settles it: **`grep -a AGENT_WORKER bio-plane/src/` RETURNS NOTHING** — an interface whose whole reason for being separate from I6 is that trust runs BOTH ways has **one implemented end**, so STABLE would be a claim about a direction no code takes. **DEC-65 ANSWERED (a) HELD, recommendation AMENDED not declined — and FL-3 FOUND THE DECISION ENTRY'S OWN C-NUMBER WAS WRONG.** The rule is `C-25.6` / `VERSION_GROUND_UNASSERTED`; **`C-25.15` is `VERSION_ORPHAN_ROW` and unrelated**, so building shape (b) as the entry worded it **would have edited the wrong check.** Caught only because FL-3 went to the check before it went to the edit. **AND (b) IS A TWO-SITE CHANGE WHOSE SECOND SITE FIRES FIRST:** PL-3's endpoint guard refuses on `legsIn.length > 0` — any leg at all, regardless of parts — so amending `C-25.6` alone **unblocks nothing, and a change built against the entry as written would have landed, passed its own suite, and changed no behaviour.** FL-3 declined to build past a vocabulary question it judged PL-1's: a machine's single-part ground row would carry `asserted_by: class:ai` **in a field whose published meaning is *a member said this part is enough on its own*** — the honest shape is a third state, and minting one is IS-1's. **Meanwhile the consequence is EXERCISED rather than survived:** refused by name → routed to ADJUST → unanswerable → **candidate DROPPED with `repeats` at zero**, and §9's `level-empty` produced deterministically by the table, **so an empty-handed background run is countable rather than silent.** Battery **123 suites / 122 green + 1 named skip at 7,659**, +203 attributed by re-running the true baseline with the tree verified byte-identical afterwards; `--strict` exit 0, unchanged, with `agent-worker` now carrying two suites; UI harness exit 0; **DEC-49 guard unmoved and no floor owed, because it walks `bio-plane/src` and `checks` and this item touched neither.** **10 new control arms plus FL-2's 12 re-run, and FIVE came back wrong first — every one a finding about the instrument: (1) H1 NEVER ARMED**, its patch matching zero times, reported as a finding rather than as the green run underneath it; **(2) H1 then exited on a RESTORE MISMATCH, because it took two snapshots of one file and named its copy from the PATH ALONE, so the second overwrote the first — and `cmp` CAUGHT WHAT THE SHA256 COULD NOT, which is the entire argument for two instruments** rather than one; **(3) H1's MUST-NOT declaration was wrong** for a real coupling — `queue` is dedup's OWN OUTPUT, so skipping dedup means nothing is submitted, no refusal is earned and F10 has nothing to route — re-declared against families sharing no state; **(4) H2 and H9 KILLED the suite rather than failing it (`0 pass, -1 FAIL`), visible ONLY because of the `-1` convention, and the class was then swept across BOTH suites — which exposed that the new arms had regressed FL-2's A3 the same way**; and **(5) A3's patch string went stale**, caught by *an arm that did not arm is a finding*. **What it states it CANNOT see, and it is the most useful part of the report: the plane in its suite is a MOCK reproduced FROM READING THE SOURCE** — `aiTaskScope`, PL-3's real six checks and F10 key, PL-5's real terminate, PL-9's real rows, PL-12's real spawn payload — **so both sides are separately green and NOTHING IN THIS ITEM PROVES THEY AGREE END TO END IN ONE PROCESS**; only a live run in a scratch namespace closes it, and that needs DS-4. **No model turn was ever run** (`turns_run: 0`, every judgement caller-supplied), the `runtime` spend is a PROXY of one unit per plane call and not a platform measurement, and `MAX_STEPS = 400` is a guard against a table defect spinning an isolate — **deliberately never reported as a run's bound.**

- **M0-17** — **AN ID IS NOW TAKEN BY AN `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` CLAIM FILE IN THE ONE `.git` ALL SIXTY WORKTREES SHARE, AND THE ORDER OF THE TWO LAYERS IS THE DESIGN. Landed 2026-08-08.** The corpus floor — today's read-the-file convention — **is KEPT but DEMOTED from *the allocator* to *the number an allocation may not go below*.** The exclusive create is the part that cannot be raced: **of two processes attempting it exactly one wins, and there is no window between check and act BECAUSE THERE IS NO CHECK.** **THE SCOPE IS THE MEASUREMENT: every colliding pair that day was two worktrees of ONE CLONE, and `--git-common-dir` is exactly that scope — the same fact `refs/stash` taught this project from the painful side, used from the useful one.** **RIVALS PRICED RATHER THAN DISMISSED, and the reserved-block one lost on two MEASURED facts:** a committed ledger is not a compare-and-swap and workers are briefed not to push; ids from the agent id destroy the property ids are for; **and the reserved block — the close rival — fails because CONDUCT IS NOT THE ONLY ALLOCATOR (D-184 and D-185 both collided with BOB-session rows, and BOB is not spawned by CONDUCT)** and because it puts a per-spawn manual step on **the one serial session already recorded twice confusing REPORTING an action with DOING it.** Mechanising the renumber lost on an **asymmetry: a mint has no failure mode that produces a WRONG id, only a SKIPPED one — and two of that day's renumbers were already found defective.** **THE RACE IS DRIVEN AND LIVES IN THE BATTERY, NOT IN A REPORT: eight real processes at the standing budget, started together, one ledger — 47 ms of EVERY-CHILD-ALIVE OVERLAP ASSERTED, because eight distinct ids from eight SERIALISED children would prove nothing** — 7 of 8 reported losing a take and stepping over it, eight distinct ids. **Neutered (`wx` → `w`), the same driver gave ALL EIGHT THE SAME NUMBER: the collision reproduced on demand, which nobody had done for the seven real ones.** **THE SWEEP FOUND SIXTEEN NAMESPACES, NOT FOUR** — the queue "item id" is not one namespace but **ten sibling families sharing a corpus** — split by how each is REFERENCED: code-referenced (catalog, suites, a harvester, a regex literal), prose-referenced (**including claims and reports written by sessions that have ENDED**), and structural (a contract identity two areas build against). **They collide identically and are protected identically; what differs per namespace is only WHERE ITS FLOOR IS READ FROM.** **And it named the precedent that was there all along: the PLANE already refuses to allocate by reading and incrementing — `Store.allocId` runs inside a transaction. The RUNTIME namespace was mechanised long ago; the DEVELOPMENT ones were the gap.** **TWO ARMS CAME BACK WRONG AND THE SECOND IS THE DAY'S BEST RECEIPT: THE TOOL CAUGHT ITS OWN DOCUMENTATION POISONING ITS OWN CORPUS, MINUTES AFTER LANDING.** The debt row explaining the gap cost carried **a worked example naming the next free number**, in `DEBT.md`, which is D's own corpus — **so `--list` read a floor off the prose. That is the C-29 catalogue comment's lesson arriving a SECOND time on the same day, in a different file, to a different author.** The generous floor stands (**over-counting costs a gap, under-counting costs a collision, and only one is recoverable**), and a per-namespace allocation pattern is read beside it so a prose-driven floor is **named with the gap it will cost** and answers **`null` — never 0** — where a namespace has not declared one. **Arm (7) exists because the instrument found it, not because anyone designed it.** The other wrong arm **reached its declared verdict by THROWING OUT OF THE MODULE** — `null pass, null fail`, no assertion involved, D-93's shape inside a control, shown only by the FOOT sentinel. **`kickoffs/CONDUCT.md` step 1 edited and NOTHING ELSE, and `mintid.test.mjs` ASSERTS THAT FILE NAMES THE TOOL — so the mechanism cannot silently leave the loop**, which is the rule *a mechanism not in the loop the reader runs is not a mechanism* enforced against itself. **What it costs when it fails, stated: GAPS — a number existing no longer implies the one below it does; a lost ledger degrades to today's convention AND NO FURTHER; two clones or a non-atomic FS bring collisions back (D-242, where the real cost is the CONFIDENCE not the collision); and nothing detects an id taken WITHOUT the tool (D-243), whose fix is a step in CONDUCT's loop rather than a suite, because the ledger is in no commit a suite could read.** Battery **132/132 at 8,256**; `--strict` exit 0; UI harness exit 0. **CONDUCT VERIFIED THE TOOL RATHER THAN TAKING IT ON REPORT: `--list` answers off the real ledger in `.git/bio-idalloc`, reading 770 `C` references and 1,568 `D` references.**
**AT INTEGRATION: the floor block was collapsed to ONE key set for the FOURTH time** — eleven items have now moved it in parallel — with every worker's figure and the warning kept where the next resolver reads them. **And CONDUCT integrated this item BEFORE spawning, against its own SPAWN-FIRST rule, for a stated reason: every brief written after it should carry the tool, so integrating first is what makes the next spawn correct rather than a fifth collision.** A rule with a named exception is still a rule; an unnamed one is a habit.

(19), `substrate` (18), `caller-owned` (4) and `observational` (1) are not that. **THE OTHER EIGHTEEN ARE GENUINELY UNDETERMINED, AND THE GAP THEY NAME IS IN THE LADDER RATHER THAN THE TABLE.** They share one shape: performed once, kept attributed and dated, corrected by a further act moving FORWARD — **and NOT SIGNED.** DEC-19 named exactly that property (*cannot be undone SILENTLY*) and attached it to `attested`, the rung requiring a key. **These have the property without the key, so `attested` would claim a signature that does not exist and `reversible` a way back that does not.** against the publishing route rather than against a string. **A NEW OP CANNOT ARRIVE UNCLASSIFIED: the op set comes through M0-12's EXISTING dispatch reader grown by one field — not a second mechanism printed-and-floored reach and the BACKWARD direction, and recorded in the suite. **That is the third time this week a totality claim has passed over nothing.** **Two instrument defects found and recorded:** a method-body reader that took `indexOf("{")` and landed on a **DESTRUCTURING brace**, reporting no justification refusal for six ops that plainly have one — caught only because the worker had read those refusals by eye first; and **six version acts sharing ONE `#moveVersionState` with ONE refusal that fires for only TWO of them, so a textual classifier reports 6 where the truth is 2** attributed by diffing two full runs; `--strict` exit 0; UI harness exit 0; no governed region touched.

THE PLANE MEETS THE SAME GUARANTEE STRUCTURALLY — WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY THE LEDGER WENT DARK UNNOTICED.** So it was a **second account of an absence beside a machine-kept one, with no rule saying which is authoritative** — D-69's diffusion through a different door. **Measured on the running code through miniflare, not read off a comment.** **AND IT IS WORSE THAN C-8.1's, WHICH IS WHAT SETTLED IT: C-7.1 VALIDATED THE SHAPE OF A DELETION CLAIM AND NOTHING ABOUT ITS TRUTH.** A ledger naming `bundle.md` as deleted **while `bundle.md` sat in the bundle** drew nothing; a `preserved_to` pointing nowhere drew nothing; records out of order in an *append-only* ledger drew nothing. **An absence is exactly the claim a reader cannot check from the bundle** — so the check offered assurance precisely where it had none. **WHOSE ACT WOULD IT RECORD? A slot finishing early **pulls the next item on ITS OWN column forward rather than idling**; the columns are ordered queues, not lockstep pairs.

### INTEGRATION ORDER — D-271 AND UI-43 ARE COUPLED. READ BEFORE MERGING EITHER.

**Both are green on their own branches and merging them in the wrong order ships a surface
that the plane refuses.** D-271 gives `op=versionaccept` a required `affirmed` argument
(C-25.33) whenever the reading declares more than one part. UI-43's accept ceremony is
UNMERGED, calls `versionaccept` once, and **already holds `affirmed: {label: true}` with
nowhere to send it** — so the wiring is ONE LINE.

- **Merge D-271 first and UI-43 unchanged** → every multi-part accept the ceremony makes is
  refused by name, and two of its page sentences (that the affirmation and the derivation
  are not recorded) become FALSE while still rendering.
- **The fix is UI's and it is small**: send the field, and correct those two sentences.
  D-271 deliberately edited nothing under `civicos-ui/`, which is right.

**So: land UI-43 with the one-line wiring and the two corrected sentences in the same turn
as D-271, or land D-271 and immediately queue the UI correction as the next item — but do
not merge D-271 and leave the ceremony as it stands.** Filed as IC-52's sequencing.

**D-271's other two findings for whoever integrates:**
- **`released:` in `CLAIMS.md` IS NOT A LIVENESS SIGNAL.** A sweep read 38 live claims; PL-14
  and M0-13 are merged on `main` with EMPTY `released:` lines, and many blocks carry TWO
  `released:` lines — a filled one followed by an empty template one — which makes 56 read
  as live. Check liveness against `git log --all`, not against this field.
- **An instrument allocated a check out of a COMMENT** — `coverage.mjs` harvests `C-\d+\.\d+`
  over raw source, so naming a skipped numeral in prose made the catalog read 225 against 224
  and took `--strict` to exit 1. **Spawned as D-277**; it is the fourth outing of that class
  and the first on a GATE.

### WAVE SPAWNED 2026-08-10 — FOUR WORKERS, and every row below existed before its worker did

**The handoff said FOUR branches were unmerged. There were SIX**, and the two it did not name
carried real content absent from `main` — D-270 (`test/d270-reach.test.mjs`, 458 lines) and
D-263 (`test/register-grammar.test.mjs`). Both were checked for CONTENT rather than for the
word "merged": `git cat-file -e origin/main:<a file the item added>` answered ABSENT for each.
**A handoff is a claim, not a measurement**, and this is the `--is-ancestor`/content asymmetry
arriving in prose instead of in git.

**Also corrected here rather than left to rot:** PL-17, PL-18, PL-19, PL-20, D-269 and D-271
all read `queued` or `running` while their work is on `main`. Content-checked, one by one —
`airun.mjs` carries DEC-63's gate, `store.mjs` carries `affirmed` at 22 sites, `C-25.33` is in
the catalog. **A status field nobody re-reads goes stale exactly the way a hand-carried number
does**, and this file is the one every other session reads to learn what is running.

**Why these four, and why not more:** the IS build plan is drained to W9 — every PL, FL, SK, VF
and UI row it schedules is landed by content (checked: `fence.test.mjs`, `scheduler.test.mjs`,
`fanout.test.mjs`, `publishedcase.test.mjs`, `version-review.test.mjs`, `notifications.test.mjs`
all present). **The forward work is now the DEBT residue those items raised**, and the four
below are the rows whose paths are DISJOINT. **D-281 is deliberately NOT spawned:** closing it
needs one honest 8-battery run on a QUIET machine, and a wave is the opposite of that — it
would measure this wave rather than the ceiling, which is the instrument-answering-about-itself
class this project keeps paying for.

| **D-251** | a text layer's fidelity is unmeasured and the one cheap read that narrows it is unbuilt — 3 of 14 Legistar attachments name ABBYY in `/Info` | CONTENT-PDF (promoted) | `pdfstructure.mjs`, I2 |

### PL-17 · done (status corrected 2026-08-10 by CONDUCT: PL-19 depends-on it and records it `done`, and PL-19 itself merged at 7c94b43)

depends-on: none — PL-1 landed and this extends its field
scope: **DEC-65's enactment, step one of three, and the ruling's own amendment is that this comes FIRST.** Mint a THIRD `asserted_by` state: an explicit **"no independent-sufficiency claim was made"** value, distinct both from a member's affirmative claim and from a silent default. **The defect it removes is an overclaim in a field's PUBLISHED MEANING** — FL-3 measured that a machine's single-part ground row would carry `asserted_by: class:ai` in a field that publishes as *"a member said this part is enough on its own"*, so the record would attribute to a member a claim no member made. This is *undetermined is first-class and must be STATED* applied to one field. DEC-32 governs the semantics being extended and the third state must not become a back door to independent sufficiency: the default is AND, and sufficiency is only ever reached by an affirmative, attributed act. No AND/OR vocabulary reaches any member-facing surface.
NOT in scope, per DEC-65's sequencing: `C-25.6` and PL-3's endpoint guard, which change TOGETHER in the item owning those files (the DELEGATION is filed in `CLAIMS.md`); and PL-14's re-measurement of the strength pair over grounds, which follows. **FL-3 measured that PL-3's guard refuses on `legsIn.length > 0` and therefore fires FIRST, so amending the check alone would land, pass its own suite, and change no behaviour** — which is the reason these are sequenced rather than parallel.
accepts-when: the third state exists and is reachable; a machine's single-part row carries it rather than a member's meaning; the battery is green; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` exit 0 read unpiped; the worker states whether existing rows need a migration.

depends-on: none — runnable now
scope: **DEC-63's enactment, and it is the one of the four that CHANGES SHIPPED BEHAVIOUR.** Bob ruled 2026-08-09 that an investigation can be started by **any member of the project**: the gate is PROJECT MEMBERSHIP, not a capability tier. The three run verbs currently check `contribute` (IS-6's provisional). **The refusal must NAME WHICH of the two failed.** *"You are not a member of this project"* and *"you lack contribute"* are different facts about a member, and one refusal covering both tells them nothing they can act on — that is DEC-49's rule (a code with a canned translation, the code a STRING LITERAL at its site through the `refusal` helper) and the ACT-AND-SAY principle in the same breath. ### M0-13 · done
milestone: M0
scope: **DIAGNOSE D-231 — `bio-plane/test/suggest.test.mjs` IS INTERMITTENTLY RED UNDER THE BATTERY AND GREEN STANDALONE.** Seen twice by two sessions that could not see each other: PL-14 measured 60/1 once and 61/0 on a re-run plus three standalone runs, from a tree carrying none of its files; CONDUCT measured **59/2 under the battery**, then 61/0 on three consecutive standalone runs and a clean full battery, from a different tree. **The same two arms both times:** CHECK 3 (a reading identical in substance to one already held is REFUSED by C-number and the reading it matches is NAMED) and the structural arm behind it (*ONE refusal row exists for three submissions*). **The obvious suspect is F10's verbatim-resubmit key** — keyed on the canonical submission BYTE FOR BYTE plus the inquiry's `bundle_sha`, which is the one value in that suite whose freshness a concurrently-running suite could move; a stale-or-fresh key is exactly the difference between a stored refusal being returned and the checks being re-run. **THE SUSPECT IS A STARTING POINT AND NOT THE ANSWER — name the cause, do not confirm the guess.** **WHY THIS OUTRANKS ITS SIZE: an intermittently red suite is one whose GREEN carries no information, and a battery that is intermittently red trains every session to re-run rather than investigate — which is how a real regression gets attributed to the flake.** This project's entire verification posture rests on the battery being believed, and D-93 is already the receipt for what happens when a suite's failures become unreadable. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS, do not fix the one arm:** which other suites hold state ACROSS submissions or across a process boundary, and which of those could a concurrently-running suite move? `sandbox.mjs` owns `$TMPDIR` per process (D-186) — establish whether that isolation actually holds under the battery's concurrency, and say what your instrument can and cannot see about it. **DO NOT CLOSE THIS BY RE-RUNNING UNTIL IT IS GREEN, and do not silence the arm:** a flaky arm silenced is a rule nobody is enforcing and nobody remembers deleting.

### REC-75 · done
milestone: M9
scope: **D-234 — THE SUBSTANCE GATE IS DEFEATED BY A QUOTATION MARK, WITH NO CLOCK INVOLVED AND NO FLAKINESS TO WARN ANYBODY.** M0-13 has just fixed the clock half of this gate and MEASURED this half beside it, by driving the plane rather than reading it: `suggestVersion` builds its candidate composition **from RAW ARGS**, while the bytes it compares against went through `#fmSafe` — which rewrites `"` and `\` to `'`, folds newlines to spaces and trims. **So the composition derived from what was WRITTEN can never equal the composition derived from what was SUBMITTED whenever a description, claim or ground carries a quote, a backslash or trailing space.** Driven, all inside one second so the clock cannot be the cause: duplicates carrying a quote, a backslash, or trailing spaces **all LANDED.** **THIS IS THE RECORD CLAIMING MORE THAN IT CAN SUPPORT, which `CLAUDE.md` ranks above a missing feature:** §6 rule 8 says *a run adds its output as a new version ONLY IF it differs in substance from every existing one*, and a reader looking at two versions is entitled to conclude they differ. Two readings identical in substance, one of them merely punctuated differently, is that conclusion made false — **and unlike the clock defect there is no intermittency to make anyone look.** **M0-13 DID NOT FIX IT ON PURPOSE and named why: the honest fix changes what the endpoint PUBLISHES.** Comparing normalised-to-normalised means the candidate must be normalised before comparison, and then what the caller gets back in `composition` is not the bytes it sent. **Its rider is the sharper half and must be settled with it: the published `composition` can ALREADY differ from what the record holds** — so this is not a new divergence being introduced, it is an existing one being made visible and named. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS: every site comparing caller-derived bytes against stored bytes, and which side each normalises.** M0-13 swept the *clock* class and found exactly one instance plus two deliberate closures; this is the same question one axis over, and its sweep is a different one.
