### M0-15 · done
milestone: M0
scope: **AN UNTRACKED `.test.mjs` APPEARED IN A WORKER'S WORKTREE, WAS DISCOVERED AND RUN BY THE BATTERY, AND WAS INVISIBLE TO `git status` — SO A WORKER MEASURED A BASELINE THAT INCLUDED A SUITE IN NO COMMIT.** Found 2026-08-08 by REC-68, and found the only way it could be: **its first baseline discovered 124 suites including `machinefences-dec49.test.mjs` (57 pass) — REC-64's file, untracked, in no commit, and GONE BY THE NEXT RUN.** REC-68 re-established its baseline by stashing and re-running at HEAD (123 suites / 7,752, restore verified by sha256 AND `cmp`) and **could not determine how the file entered its worktree.** **WHY THIS OUTRANKS ITS SIZE, and it is the same argument as D-231's: EVERY WORKER IN THIS PROJECT IS INSTRUCTED TO MEASURE ITS OWN BASELINE AND TRUST IT OVER ITS BRIEF** — that instruction is now on nine consecutive items and has caught a stale figure every time. **A baseline that silently includes a phantom suite makes that instruction produce a WRONG number with full confidence**, which is strictly worse than the stale briefs it was written to defeat: a stale brief is corrected by measurement, and this defeats measurement itself. **And the delta arithmetic every item reports — `+N attributed by re-running the true baseline` — is computed against it.** **THE MECHANISM IS THE ITEM AND IT IS NOT YET KNOWN.** Candidates to eliminate rather than assume: `.worktreeinclude` (which legitimately carries `.env` into worktrees, so it is a copying mechanism that already exists); worktrees living at `.claude/worktrees/` INSIDE the repository, so a walk or a runner rooted at the repo root can reach into a sibling worktree; a harness or probe writing outside its own tree; and the shared scratchpad, which has already overwritten one worker's control harness mid-turn (ORCHESTRATION's failure table). **NAME THE MECHANISM; do not confirm the first candidate** — M0-13 and M0-10 were each handed an obvious suspect and both suspects were wrong. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS: what else can enter a worktree without a commit, and what else does the battery DISCOVER rather than being told about?** `scripts/battery.mjs` discovers by filename, and CPDF-9 has just taught it to discover fleet members by manifest — **discovery is now the runner's main mechanism, and discovery over an uncontrolled directory is the exposure.** **The fix must make the phantom VISIBLE, not merely absent:** a battery that silently skips untracked suites would hide the next one. It should say what it ran and whether every suite it ran is in a commit.
behind-interface: none — test estate and the runner; it changes no plane behaviour
depends-on: none
accepts-when: **the MECHANISM named** in `battery.mjs`'s own header and in the debt row's disposition, not the symptom suppressed; `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline and trust it over this brief, and note that establishing whether YOUR baseline is honest is this item's subject, so state how you checked it**; any delta attributed per suite; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` from the REPO ROOT, exit read unpiped. **The battery must REPORT any suite it ran that is not in a commit, by name** — silence is the defect. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded, each armed ALONE, restores verified by sha256 AND content against uniquely-named per-arm copies, the harness INSIDE your own worktree — (1) **plant an untracked `.test.mjs` and the battery must NAME it** — the arm this item exists for, and it must name it whether the suite passes or fails, because a passing phantom is the case that actually happened; (2) **re-arm the mechanism you named and the phantom must REAPPEAR**, which is what turns a hypothesis into a diagnosis; (3) neuter the check and its reach must fail as a DELTA with the corpus PRINTED; (4) an over-strictness arm — a legitimately tracked suite, and a legitimately untracked NON-suite file, must both pass without a word.
added: 2026-08-08 · CONDUCT (REC-68's finding, routed at its integration; it could not determine the mechanism and said so, which is why this is an item rather than a note)
disposition: **DONE 2026-08-08. THE MECHANISM IS NAMED AND IT IS `git stash`: THE STASH STACK IS REPOSITORY-WIDE, NOT PER-WORKTREE.** MEASURED, not inferred — a `git stash push` inside worktree `agent-a0e79024273135242` wrote `refs/stash` and `logs/refs/stash` into the COMMON git directory `bio/.git/` while that worktree's own `refs/` stayed EMPTY. **All 60 checkouts share ONE stack**, so `stash@{0}` means *what any of the sixty pushed last*, and `push -u` carries UNTRACKED files. **THE INSTANCE IS IN THE OBJECT DATABASE** and was verified there rather than taken on report: stash `8706832`, headed `On worktree-agent-a773e28c7c7d0fb8b: RESTORED BY UI-50 SESSION: another session's D-228 work, accidentally popped from stash by ui50`, contains a DIFFERENT worker's `src/query.mjs`, `test/search.test.mjs` and `test/meaningquery.test.mjs`. **RE-ARMED AND REPRODUCED** in a scratch repository: A writes an untracked suite, A pushes, B pops, the suite lands in B byte-identical (sha256 `a10e94a7…`) — control arm (2), which is what turns a hypothesis into a diagnosis. **AND IT IS ONE MECHANISM, NOT TWO:** UI-50's reading that "another session is working in this worktree" is not required by the evidence — an interleaved push between its own push and pop produces exactly that appearance from one cause, and that correction is fed back rather than left standing. **ELIMINATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**, since M0-13 and M0-10 were each handed an obvious suspect and both were wrong: `.worktreeinclude` names two literal filenames with no glob and cannot carry a `.test.mjs`; neither battery walk can reach a sibling worktree (the plane walk does not descend, the fleet walk filters `!d.startsWith(".")`, worktrees live under `.claude/worktrees/`); the shared scratchpad is outside the repository so it can corrupt a harness but cannot enrol a suite; no harness roots a WRITE outside its own tree since M0-10; and a suite found in the MAIN checkout that is in no commit of `main` looked exactly like a leak and was CONDUCT's integration in flight (`MERGE_HEAD` present). **THE FIX MAKES THE PHANTOM VISIBLE, NOT ABSENT:** `scripts/battery.mjs` checks every suite it RAN and every fleet manifest that enrolled one against `git ls-tree HEAD` — not `git status`, which cannot see an ignored file — NAMES anything not in a commit (UNTRACKED vs staged-not-committed), prints beside the contaminated total the total another checkout reproduces, and says UNVERIFIED rather than clean when git cannot answer. **THE SWEEP FOUND TWO DISCOVERY PATHS IN THE BATTERY, NOT ONE** — filename in `test/`, and `fleet-member.json` at the repo root since CPDF-9 — and the manifest is the LARGER hole because it enrols a whole directory; both are checked. Three further walks over the same uncontrolled directory (`coverage.mjs` twice, `hygiene.test.mjs` three times) are unguarded and NAMED in D-238 rather than left silent. **BASELINE MEASURED IN THIS WORKTREE AND ITS OWN HONESTY ESTABLISHED, which is this item's subject: 124/124 green · 7,815 assertions · exit 0**, agreeing with the brief, with every one of the 121 plane suites and both fleet manifests confirmed present in the commit at HEAD by `ls-tree`. **AFTER: 125/125 green · 7,841 assertions · exit 0**, delta ATTRIBUTED per suite by re-running the baseline and diffing per-suite lines, never by subtraction — `battery-provenance.test.mjs` +23 (new), `hygiene.test.mjs` 507→509, `planning-hygiene.test.mjs` 272→273 (both corpus walks that scale with the suite count) = +26. `coverage.mjs --strict` exit 0 unpiped; REGISTER_FLOOR moved 478/120/121 → **486/121/122** in the same turn from the figures the instrument PRINTED (`GREW by 8 arm(s)`). **CONTROLS: four arms, each armed ALONE, and TWO CAME BACK WRONG — both recorded rather than smoothed.** (1) plant an untracked PASSING `.test.mjs` in this worktree → NAMED, run still green, contaminated 65 and reproducible 8 both printed, over a corpus of 166 tracked files. (2) re-arm the mechanism → phantom REAPPEARED byte-identical. (3) neuter the naming branch → 14 pass / **9 fail** over a printed corpus of 7 scratch repositories; **DECLARED 8, GOT 9**, the ninth being the both-totals assertion which also lives in that branch. (4) over-strictness, held open throughout → a tracked suite, an untracked `.md`, an untracked `.mjs` helper and an untracked harness directory all pass without a word. **THE HARNESS FAILURE WORTH MORE THAN THE ARM:** arm (1)'s first restore check used `xargs -a`, which BSD xargs does not have, so the sha256 manifest came out EMPTY and `cmp` reported "byte-identical" over two empty files — digest `e3b0c442…`, the empty string. **A HEADLINE ASSERTION PASSING OVER AN EMPTY CORPUS**, caught only because the harness printed its corpus size; the guard is now in the harness and the note is kept at the line. **WHAT IS NOT CLOSED AND IS THE BIGGER FINDING:** the runner detects, it cannot prevent, and **the recipe every worker is briefed to use for a true baseline is `git stash`** — so the practice defending this project against stale figures is itself the delivery mechanism. `ORCHESTRATION.md` gains the failure-table row and a corrected recipe in the same turn: do not park at all (the provenance line makes a dirty-tree baseline readable), else `git worktree add` a scratch checkout, and if you stash anyway push with `-m <agent-id>`, capture the stash SHA immediately, and `apply` by SHA — never `pop`, never `stash@{0}`. D-238 carries the defect; VERIFICATION.md gains the section and the moved figures; MEASUREMENTS.md carries the stash-scope measurement and the discovery-path table.

scope: **`op=airun` PUBLISHES TWO OF THE RUN'S THREE CONDITIONS, AND THE THIRD IS SILENT — measured at the source by SK-1 while wiring the skill version, not inferred.** §11 names three conditions a run is formed under: the bias manifest in force, **the launching project's declared standard PAIR**, and the skill version. `ai_runs.standard_pair` is WRITTEN by `aiRunOpen` and PUBLISHED by `aiRunSpawnPayload` — **and `aiRunRead` does not publish it at all**, so a member reading the run object sees the skill version and the bias block and **cannot see the bar the run was working to.** That is the same shape PL-12 found one field over, where the manifest was written by one op and read by no op, so *"no manifest was in force"* was stated nowhere a reader could see: **a condition recorded and never published is not recorded for anybody who was not there.** **SK-1 DELIBERATELY DID NOT FIX IT, and the reason is the item:** the honest publication must distinguish **"no bar was in force"** from **"the bar was X"**, and under DEC-17/DEC-21 the pair is PER-PROJECT while *a projectless inquiry has no CURRENT and no bar at all* (SWEEP C8) — so the absent case is a first-class answer about the pair's semantics, not a null. **UNDETERMINED IS FIRST-CLASS AND MUST BE STATED** applies to this field exactly as to a grade. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS, do not fix the one field:** every condition and principal the run object STORES, checked against what each reader PUBLISHES — `aiRunRead`, `aiRunSpawnPayload` and any surface reading either — with the readers that disagree named. Two readers of one row disagreeing about which of its facts exist is the defect; the missing pair is one instance of it.

NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded, each armed ALONE with the restore verified by sha256 AND content, the harness INSIDE your own worktree — (1) drop the new publication and the reader arm fails naming the field; (2) publish the absent case as an empty/null value indistinguishable from a real bar, and the arm that tells the two apart must FAIL — this is the control the item exists for; (3) neuter the walk that enumerates stored-vs-published conditions and its REACH fails as a DELTA with the corpus size PRINTED; (4) an over-strictness arm — a run that legitimately has no project, and therefore no bar, must READ CLEANLY and not be refused.

scope: **`bio-plane/test/bounds.test.mjs`'s REC-59 consumer walk MISCLASSIFIES A NON-CALL-SITE, and it turned another area's suite red for a reason that had nothing to do with that area (UI-46's delegation).** The walk anchors on `(?:recR|rec|call|DO|get|j|GET)\(` immediately before `"projection"`, so **an ordinary `Map.get("projection")` in a test file is counted as a corpus-arm CALL SITE in `civicos-ui/`** — which falsified the assertion *"civicos-ui reaches op=projection ONLY through the `&id=` arm"* and took the battery from 106/106 to 105/106 **with `bio-plane/**` never opened.** UI-46 worked around it on its own side (the op name held in a constant, with the reason at the site) and routed the matcher rather than editing another area's suite. **IT FAILED ALARMING, WHICH IS THE SAFE DIRECTION — and that is exactly why it is worth fixing rather than shrugging at:** a walk that INVENTS a consumer will one day invent one that HIDES a real answer, and this file's own subject is claims that cost nothing to produce. `get(` is the offender — it is a Map/Set method name as often as a fetch verb. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** several walks now anchor on call-shaped prefixes; the question is which others admit a method name that is not a call to the plane. **State plainly what your matcher can and cannot see**, as REC-60's and UI-46's do.

milestone: M8
scope: The server-side TASK-ACTOR FENCE (lifted from UI-1's delegation). Today `taskResolve`/`taskForward` (`store.mjs` ~5299) refuse no-actor / no-such-task / already-resolved, but do NOT refuse a member who is neither the task's `assignee` nor an admin — any member-class credential can resolve or forward ANY task by id (the actor is stamped honestly into history, so it is traceable, but not PREVENTED). RECONCILE with D-98's routing doctrine before over-fencing: an `unassigned` task is meant to be claimable by the routed role — check what the routing intends (member_expertise → PM → group admin) and keep that path open; the admin override stays. scope: **D-236 — THE DEC-49 GUARD'S TEETH ARE BLIND TO EIGHT REFUSAL OBJECTS, AND IT HAS ALREADY COST A TRANSLATION.** Measured 2026-08-08 by REC-64, and found the only way it could be: **its own new governed site came back `0 judged, 0 checked`.** Arm C grades a refusal by looking for `ok: false` — **704 sites answer that way, but 5 answer `started: false` and 3 compute `ok: !x`, and all eight are invisible to it.** The consequence is not hypothetical: **`SET_MOVED` CANNOT TAKE A REGION**, because a region there judges zero and the guard would report an unfalsifiable site. **SO A REAL TRANSLATION WAS NOT WRITTEN BECAUSE THE INSTRUMENT COULD NOT SEE THE REFUSAL.** **THE FIX INVERTS, IT DOES NOT LENGTHEN A LIST** — REC-70's lesson, and this is its fourth outing: a classifier that grades only returns containing one literal hid 27 ops and read as a complete sweep, and the correction was to grade every return that does not declare itself a success. **Ask what makes something a refusal IN PRINCIPLE.** `started: false` and `ok: !x` are two spellings that exist today; the fourth will be written next week. **AND PRINT WHAT THE WALK COULD NOT CLASSIFY** — a site whose refusal shape the matcher does not understand must be NAMED, not silently scored zero, which is exactly the fix M0-14 landed for the control register and CPDF-9 landed for the dark fleet member. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS, and the class is larger than the guard: which other instruments in this repository grade a return by one literal?** REC-67 has just swept call-shaped prefixes and found three; this is the same question about success-and-failure shapes. **Print your corpus and reach; state plainly what your matcher can and cannot see.**
behind-interface: none — instrument only; it changes no plane behaviour
depends-on: none — REC-64 landed and its measurement is in the D-236 row
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline and trust it over this brief; ELEVEN consecutive items have found a briefed figure stale by measuring it**; delta attributed per suite by re-running the true baseline; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` **from the REPO ROOT**, exit read unpiped, 0, **with every floor it invalidates moved IN THE SAME TURN from the figures the guard PRINTED** — and note `regionLines` is a property of the MERGED source and has moved at integration four times. **`SET_MOVED` given its region and its translation, since that is the concrete debt this closes.** NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded, each armed ALONE, restores verified by sha256 AND content against uniquely-named per-arm copies, the harness INSIDE your own worktree — (1) **plant a refusal in a shape the matcher was never taught and it must be COUNTED or NAMED, never silently scored zero** — the arm this item exists for; (2) neuter the walk and its reach fails as a DELTA with the corpus PRINTED; (3) an over-strictness arm — a SUCCESS return in an unanticipated spelling must NOT be graded as a refusal, which is the direction that would flood the guard with false sites; (4) re-run REC-64's own arms and they must still behave, so the widening did not disturb what already worked.

### M0-17 · done
milestone: M0
scope: **MINT IDS INSTEAD OF READING THE FILE AND ADDING ONE. SEVEN ITEMS COLLIDED ON AN ID IN A SINGLE DAY** — a C-number family (PL-11 vs PL-14, C-29), two IC numbers (FL-3 vs CPDF-9 on IC-33; REC-63 vs REC-66 on IC-35), and four debt numbers (three items on D-235, two on D-237, two on D-238). **In EVERY case both workers measured the number free over the real file and BOTH WERE RIGHT WHEN THEY LOOKED.** Nobody was careless; the convention is the defect. **THIS IS A MEASURED PROPERTY OF A READ-THE-FILE-AND-ADD-ONE CONVENTION UNDER A RAISED CONCURRENCY BUDGET, not a run of accidents** — it was one collision at a budget of two, and seven at a budget of eight, and it will be worse at sixteen. **THE COST IS PAID BY THE INTEGRATOR AND IT IS NOT FREE:** each renumber is a by-the-number sweep across code, suites, claims and reports, **and one of them missed a REGEX LITERAL — where `C-29\.` is not the text `C-29.`** — caught only by the suite. Another mangled its own worked example, which `coverage.mjs` then read as a catalog check nobody names. **A vigilance fix has already been tried and it does not work: every brief for the last two days has told workers to measure before allocating, and every one of them DID.** WHAT THE ITEM IS: a way to take an id that CANNOT be taken twice. Candidates worth weighing rather than assuming — a `tools/mintid.mjs` that allocates against a committed ledger and pushes before the worker starts; ids derived from something already unique (the worker's own agent id or branch); a RESERVED BLOCK handed out in the brief at spawn time, which costs the integrator one decision per spawn and nothing afterwards; or accepting collisions and making the RENUMBER mechanical and total. **The last is a legitimate answer if it is measured to be cheaper — say which you chose and why, and what it costs when it fails.** **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS: which OTHER shared namespaces does this project allocate into by reading and incrementing?** C-numbers, IC numbers, D-numbers and queue item ids are four; there may be more, and each may need a different answer.
behind-interface: none — tooling and convention; it changes no plane behaviour
depends-on: none
accepts-when: **the mechanism WORKS UNDER CONCURRENCY, demonstrated rather than argued** — two allocations racing must produce two different ids, driven, not reasoned about; `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline and trust it over this brief** — delta attributed per suite; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` **from the REPO ROOT**, exit read unpiped, 0; `node tools/plancheck.mjs` clean but for UNPUSHED. **`kickoffs/CONDUCT.md` updated in the same turn** — a mechanism that is not in the loop the reader actually runs is not a mechanism, and this one has to be in the brief CONDUCT writes at spawn time or it reaches nobody. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded, each armed ALONE, restores verified by sha256 AND content against uniquely-named per-arm copies, harness INSIDE your own worktree — (1) **race two allocations and both must survive** — the arm this item exists for, and it must be DRIVEN because the whole class arose from a race nobody simulated; (2) neuter the mechanism and the collision must REAPPEAR, which is what turns the fix into a diagnosis; (3) an over-strictness arm — an item legitimately allocating several ids at once must not be refused or given duplicates; (4) if you chose the mechanical-renumber answer instead, its arm is that a renumber over a corpus containing a REGEX LITERAL and a two-digit suffix leaves both correct.
added: 2026-08-08 · CONDUCT (earned by seven collisions in one day; recorded at the seventh rather than noted an eighth time)
landed: (committed on `worktree-agent-a6a99245c369a9fd4`, NOT pushed and NOT merged; CONDUCT integrates) — **`tools/mintid.mjs`: A TAKE IS AN EXCLUSIVE CREATE, IN THE ONE `.git` ALL SIXTY WORKTREES SHARE.** The mechanism chosen is a two-layer allocator: a **corpus floor** (today's read-the-file convention, kept but DEMOTED from *the allocator* to *the number an allocation may not go below*) under an **`O_CREAT|O_EXCL` claim file** per id. **THE SCOPE IS THE MEASUREMENT: every colliding pair on 2026-08-08 was two WORKTREES OF ONE CLONE, and the common gitdir is exactly that scope** — the same fact `refs/stash` taught this project from the painful side (D-238), used from the useful one. **THE RESERVED BLOCK WAS THE CLOSE RIVAL AND IT LOSES ON TWO MEASURED FACTS:** CONDUCT is not the only allocator (**D-184 and D-185 both collided with BOB-SESSION rows**, and BOB is not spawned by CONDUCT), and it puts a per-spawn manual step on the one serial session already recorded twice confusing REPORTING an action with DOING it. **MECHANISING THE RENUMBER was priced, not dismissed, and loses on an ASYMMETRY: a mint has no failure mode that produces a WRONG id, only a SKIPPED one** — where two of the day's renumbers were already found defective (one missed a REGEX LITERAL, `C-29\.` is not the text `C-29.`; one mangled the worked example inside the comment warning about renumbering). **THE RACE IS DRIVEN AND IT IS IN THE BATTERY, not in a report:** eight real processes (the standing budget), started together — **47 ms of every-child-alive overlap, 7 of 8 reported LOSING a take and stepping over it, eight distinct ids** — and the suite ASSERTS the overlap, because eight distinct ids from eight serialised children would prove nothing. **THE NEUTERED ARM CLOSES IT FROM THE OTHER SIDE: with `wx` -> `w`, THE SAME DRIVER GAVE ALL EIGHT PROCESSES `D-242`** — the collision reproduced on demand, which nobody had done for the seven real ones. **THE SWEEP FOUND SIXTEEN NAMESPACES, NOT FOUR**, and the queue "item id" is not one namespace but TEN sibling families sharing a corpus: `C` (code-referenced) · `D` `DEC` `IC` `M` + `REC UI CPDF FL PL SK IS VF M0 DIST` (prose-referenced) · `I` (structural). **ONE MECHANISM, SIXTEEN CORPORA** — they collide identically and are protected identically; what differs is where each floor is read from, and that is the only per-namespace knowledge the tool holds. **AND THE FLOOR CANNOT BE READ REPOSITORY-WIDE, MEASURED: a naive repo-wide scan for `\bC-(\d+)` returns 2026**, off fixture ids shaped `…C-2024-…`, which would hand out `C-2027` and orphan the catalog permanently — so noise above a per-namespace ceiling is discarded AND NAMED. Battery **132/132 green · 8,246 assertions · 111.7 s · exit 0** against a true baseline MEASURED IN THIS WORKTREE BEFORE ANY EDIT of **131/131 · 8,209** — **the brief's figures were EXACTLY RIGHT, and that is recorded because the practice is to trust the measurement rather than the streak.** Delta **+37, attributed by DIFFING the two runs rather than by subtraction:** `mintid.test.mjs` +33 (new), `hygiene` 533 -> 535, `planning-hygiene` 276 -> 278 (the two new debt rows); every other suite byte-identical. `REGISTER_FLOOR` moved `546/127/128` -> `553/128/129` from the REPRODUCIBLE figures a green run PRINTED with every file in a commit. **SEVEN CONTROL ARMS RUN, EACH ALONE, all seven agreed** — restores verified by sha256 AND `cmp` against uniquely-named per-arm pristine copies (22,547 B / 15,865 B, digests printed and guarded against the empty-string sha256) — **and TWO INSTRUMENT FINDINGS recorded rather than quietly fixed, which is the usual direction here — the controls find the instrument wrong more often than the subject.** (a) The over-strictness arm's first run reached its declared verdict by THROWING out of the module (`null pass, null fail`, no assertion involved, D-93's class inside a control), caught ONLY by the FOOT sentinel; the bulk take is caught now and a refusal is a NAMED failure. (b) **THE TOOL CAUGHT ITS OWN DOCUMENTATION POISONING ITS OWN CORPUS, MINUTES AFTER LANDING:** the D-243 row written for this item explained the gap cost with a WORKED EXAMPLE naming the next free number, in `DEBT.md`, which is D's own corpus — and `--list` read the floor off the PROSE. **The C-29 catalogue comment's lesson arriving a second time on the same day, in a different file, to a different author.** The generous floor STANDS (over-counting costs a gap, under-counting costs a collision, and only one is recoverable); what was added is a per-namespace ALLOCATION pattern read beside it, so a prose-driven floor is NAMED with the gap it will cost, and `null` — never 0 — where a namespace has not declared one. The example is gone from every file that is a corpus. That is control arm (7), and it exists because the instrument found it rather than because anyone designed it. Residuals: **D-242** (the ledger is one clone on one machine, and the real cost is the CONFIDENCE rather than the collision) and **D-243** (nothing DETECTS an id taken without the allocator — the fix is a step in CONDUCT's integration loop, not a suite, and it is delegated rather than smuggled in). `kickoffs/CONDUCT.md` step 1 only.

### REC-77 · done
milestone: M9
scope: **`Store.#CORRESPONDENCE_RANK` OFFERS THE LEAST SELECTIVE EVIDENCE FIRST, AND M-4 MEASURED IT RATHER THAN ARGUING IT.** The rank places `name_in_reference` ABOVE `name_in_label`, so a partial-reference candidate is offered to a member before a label candidate — **and a reference term is 8.1x LESS SELECTIVE** (a term reaches 67.5% of the reference corpus against 8.3% of labels). **The sharpest single figure: the alias `"legislation"` reaches 41 of 41 references and 0 of 41 labels.** A member who registered `Legislation` as an alias of the committee would be offered **every reference in the document** as a correspondence. **THAT IS THE RECORD OFFERING MORE THAN IT CAN SUPPORT, which `CLAUDE.md` ranks above a missing feature — and it is offered FIRST, which is the ordering a member trusts most.** **A RANK SWAP IS NOT THE FIX, AND M-4 SAID SO RATHER THAN SHIPPING THE OBVIOUS ONE: THE CLASS IS BIMODAL.** `"legislation 26-0844"` reaches **1 of 41** — the source's own identifier, respelled around a punctuation mark `#normAlias` does not fold, which is a genuinely good correspondence. `"legislation"` reaches **41 of 41** and corresponds to nothing. **Both are `name_in_reference`; swapping the rank would demote the good one along with the bad.** **What separates them is SELECTIVITY, and it is measurable AT READ TIME** — that is the item. **WHAT M-4's MEASUREMENT DOES AND DOES NOT LICENSE, and honouring the limit is part of the work:** one corpus, one doctype, one institution, one reader, one kind value, so **every selectivity figure is CORPUS-RELATIVE** and a threshold hard-coded from this document's numbers would be a hand-carried figure of exactly the kind this project has been bitten by most. **No entity registry was sampled BECAUSE THERE IS NO REAL ONE** — the 41/41 blast is what WOULD happen, not something observed. **So a fix that computes selectivity over the corpus in front of it is honest; a fix that pins M-4's percentages is not.** **AND THE NO-GRADE POSTURE IS VINDICATED AND MUST NOT BE DISTURBED** — REC-40's third tier ranks lowest and carries no grade, and M-4 strengthened the reason: reference strings here are machine-composed `kind:key` from a closed two-value vocabulary, so **a partial match inside one is not a weak name correspondence, it is not a name correspondence at all.** Read `MEASUREMENTS.md`'s M-4 section in full before designing, and **re-run its probe rather than trusting its numbers** — the instrument is committed and re-runnable for exactly that reason.
behind-interface: I3 — if what a member is OFFERED changes, that is a consumer-visible change; file the IC row and let CONDUCT take the version bump
depends-on: M-4 (landed; its probe and its gate are committed)
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline and trust it over this brief; TWELVE consecutive items have found a briefed figure stale by measuring it** — delta attributed per suite by re-running the true baseline; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` **from the REPO ROOT**, exit read unpiped, 0; **every floor you invalidate moved IN THE SAME TURN from PRINTED figures.** The bimodal pair DRIVEN as two cases over one corpus — the good identifier still offered, the vacuous alias not — **and the discriminator computed from the corpus rather than pinned to M-4's percentages, asserted as such.** NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded, each armed ALONE, restores verified by sha256 AND content against uniquely-named per-arm copies, harness INSIDE your own worktree — (1) **neuter the discriminator and the vacuous alias must be offered again**, naming it; (2) **the OVER-STRICTNESS arm is the one this item turns on — the 1-of-41 identifier must STILL be offered**, because a fix that suppresses it has traded a false offer for a lost one and is the worse trade on a record whose product is trustworthiness; (3) pin the discriminator to a literal threshold instead of computing it, and an arm must FAIL naming the corpus-relative rule; (4) neuter any walk you add and its reach fails as a DELTA with the corpus PRINTED.

### M-4 · done
milestone: M9
scope: **The partial-reference tier ships UNMEASURED, and it is out-of-band measurement work rather than a build (routed out of REC-40).** REC-40's third tier ranks lowest and carries NO grade, which is the honest posture — but the number behind it does not exist: REC-36's n=41 variance probe was run over **labels**, not over reference strings, and nobody has established that reference strings vary the same way. CLAUDE.md's rule is that numbers come from `MEASUREMENTS.md` with their date and instrument, and that a claim inherited from a neighbouring population is not a measurement. Re-run `label-variance-probe.mjs` over REFERENCE STRINGS, record n, the instrument and the date, and state plainly what the number does and does not license. **If the variance turns out to be materially different from labels', say so loudly** — REC-40 shipped this tier ranked lowest precisely so that finding would be cheap to act on, and the ranking is the thing that would change. This item holds NO area slot (the measurement lane), so it can run beside two active areas.
behind-interface: none (measurement only; no op, field, shape or surface changes)
depends-on: none
accepts-when: a dated `MEASUREMENTS.md` entry naming the instrument, the population, n, and the result, written so a reader can tell it apart from REC-36's label measurement rather than mistaking one for the other; **an explicit statement of what the number does NOT establish** (CLAUDE.md's "undetermined is first-class and must be STATED"); and either a recorded finding that REC-40's ranking stands, or a queued item to change it with the measurement as the evidence. No code change is required and none should be made to force a result.
added: 2026-08-04 · CONDUCT (routed out of REC-40's measurement bound)

milestone: M7
scope: **Close op=bootstrap's unconsumed roster disclosure (REC-39's measurement; **D-198**, renumbered three times — D-184 and D-185 both collided with BOB-session rows).** An unauthenticated classes-null op answers every credentialled role plus the date each password was last set, and NOTHING consumes it (not setup.mjs, not civicos-ui, not newgroup, no battery assertion — measured). Removing a published field from an I3 op is an INTERFACE-CHANGES matter: file the IC, measure the nil consumer impact as the evidence, remove the roles field (or gate it), and REVISIT the login-distinguishability reasoning written at Store.LOGIN_REFUSAL_DETAIL in the same turn — the two travel together (with the roster closed, distinct BAD_PASSWORD/NO_SUCH_ROLE becomes the enumeration surface, and the constant's own comment says to reopen it).

landed: (merge on main, worker 21400cd) — THE PREMISE WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE FIELD AND LOOSE ABOUT THE OP, corrected in IC-20 rather than left: nothing consumes `roles` (setup.mjs calls op=bootstrap three times and reads five other fields; civicos-ui never calls the op at all; no battery assertion named it) — but `newgroup` IS a consumer of the OP at src/index.mjs:364 and :631, its version probes, which REC-41's own scope and D-188 both deny. The conclusion stands because it is a consumer of the op and not of the field, and the item's brief said to re-measure rather than inherit the claim, which is why this was caught. A near-consumer worth knowing: setup.mjs's panel row labelled "Roles with passwords" was always filled from the SIGNED-IN role and never from this field — corrected to say what it shows. **THE DISTINGUISHABILITY DECISION WAS REVERSED, NOT INHERITED**, and it is the half that would have been lost if this had been split into a later item. REC-39 kept BAD_PASSWORD and NO_SUCH_ROLE distinct on ONE recorded ground — the roster was already open, so collapsing them "would defend nothing." That ground is now gone. Three measurements decided it: op=login is classes:null and carries NO rate limit (only op=knock meters anyone), so distinct codes are an unmetered anonymous oracle — the RETAIL form of the wholesale disclosure just removed; this plane already collapses every other unauthenticated identity probe it has (#INVITE_MISS, NOT_PUBLISHED, and NO_SUCH_ROLE's own two arms); and the sole consumer of the distinction was setup.mjs's paraphrase, which stated the disclosure more plainly than the plane did. One code `SIGN_IN_REFUSED`, one sentence, from every arm, with the reasoning and the cost of reversal recorded AT the constant where the next session will look. **AND THE COLLAPSE WAS DEFEATED BY A STOPWATCH UNTIL IT WAS CLOSED — a finding not in the item and not reachable by review**: the arms that refuse WITHOUT checking a password returned instantly while a wrong password ran PBKDF2 100k, measured at 0.6ms vs 6.7ms, ELEVEN-FOLD, enumerating the live roster with any password at all. Equalised, and stated honestly at the site and in IC-20 as *dominant cost equalised, constant time NOT proved*. The worker also declines to claim member identity is now secret — op=publishedcase publishes attestor.member anonymously by design. ### CPDF-9 · done
milestone: M2
scope: **Measure whether OCR is reachable at all, before anything is designed.** D-152, DEC-4 as twice amended. Bob overruled the accept-the-limit recommendation: image-only PDFs must be extracted AND investigated for meaningful content. Unknown and gating: a WASM OCR engine's bundle size against the Worker limit, its CPU against the isolate ceiling (D-36, D-56), and its accuracy on a REAL Oakland scanned exhibit rather than a clean synthetic. **FOUR placements to decide between** — in-plane Tier 1 · the pdf-worker fleet member (I6) · an external SERVICE (a first-class candidate, not a fallback; price its distinct cost: the transcription becomes a third-party claim we cannot re-run once they change their model, so the record names the service identity and date exactly as it names an engine and version) · a service-plus-AI-post-processing chain. **Measure DIGITS specifically** — where OCR fails and where human skimming fails too, which is what the attestation ceiling turns on. **One cheap extra measurement**: detect whether a PDF's text layer was itself machine-generated (producer metadata routinely names the scanner or OCR software) — nothing looks today, and a publisher's own OCR saved as a searchable PDF is somebody else's unverified transcription (DEC-4's third amendment). Grade VALUES wait on this measurement; the doctrine (fidelity bounds the CAPTURE axis; no machine mints the grade; member attestation is the only route to the top) is already stated and the measurement must not be read as permission. COMMITS NO PRODUCT CODE; holds no slot. Record in `MEASUREMENTS.md` with date and instrument.
behind-interface: none — it commits no code
depends-on: none

### CPDF-10 · queued
milestone: M2
scope: **The Tier-3 OCR path, behind whatever placement CPDF-9's measurement permits.** PLACEMENT RE-BASED BY DEC-35 (2026-08-04, superseding the 2026-08-03 service-first note): in-plane and pdf-worker stay RULED OUT by bundle size; the IN-ACCOUNT path is the DEFAULT and its engine is now TESSERACT AS A FLEET MEMBER pending CPDF-12's deployed probe (DEC-42: Moondream returned NO-GO on coordinates; tesseract was never blocked on size, only on the Free CPU ceiling Paid removes) — sovereign instances must not need a second vendor account (the D-115 class); the EXTERNAL service (Azure DI Read primary) is the ESCALATION tier or the fallback on NO-GO; NOTHING FUNDED. Per-region confidence becomes confidence-WHERE-SUPPLIED, else a stated confidence: none in the chain with the fidelity cap set by measurement; pseudo-confidence (a self-reported number thresholded as calibrated) FORBIDDEN as the costs-nothing class; measured self-refusal (CPDF-11's degradation ladder) is the only earnable per-region trigger. The provenance chain and the image-region anchor UNCHANGED, non-negotiable. Deps gain CPDF-11 and (on GO) CPDF-12. D-152, DEC-4 as twice amended. THE PROVENANCE RULE IS THE ITEM, not the engine: **`text_source` records a CHAIN, not a token** — `pixels → ocr(engine, version) → ai(function, version) → attested(member, date, extent)` — each step naming what performed it, and **each step can only weaken the claim, never strengthen it** (an AI that cleans a garbled line produced more READABLE text, not more RELIABLE text; the hazard of this capability is output that looks better than its input — do not let the chain collapse to a single label). A text LAYER is itself an unverified transcription (`pdfstructure.mjs` already decodes through the file's own `/ToUnicode` map), so **the ceiling is VERIFIED AGAINST THE RENDERED IMAGE, reachable from both paths**: member attestation is offered on a text layer too, SCOPED to what was actually checked (a leg citing outside the attested extent does not inherit it); the chain is still recorded — verification supersedes it as grade determinant, never as record. Attestation is a member act refusable to a machine credential. Transcription fidelity BOUNDS the capture axis (weakest link of byte provenance and fidelity) — no third scale, no new machinery. A basis leg resting on OCR'd text carries its image region (page + rect); OCR never raises a capture grade; a low-confidence region reads `undetermined`, never a best guess. Text reaches the READING path via FW-15's wire.
behind-interface: I2
depends-on: CPDF-9, COFF-1, CPDF-11, CPDF-12-on-GO (dependency corrected 2026-08-03: the handover's "CPDF-8" was RECONCILED §3.3's name for the FORMAT registry, carried as COFF-1. The page-to-pixels rendering path BOB flagged as the other candidate reading is real but is DECIDED by CPDF-9's placement measurement — an external-service placement needs no renderer; an in-plane or fleet placement does — so the renderer item is named when that recommendation lands, not pre-built.)
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green with a real image-only Oakland PDF yielding text whose `text_source` chain names each step with per-region confidence and reaching `reading_refs`, while a text-layer PDF yields its own honest chain and the two are distinguishable in the projection, the index and an export; an attestation is refused to a machine credential and scoped to its extent; negative control — strip the `text_source` marker and the suite fails naming an OCR'd document indistinguishable from a published text layer; drop the confidence floor so a garbled region emits a best guess and the suite fails; collapse the chain to one label and the suite fails.

### CPDF-11 · done
milestone: M2
scope: **Measure Moondream 3.1 (Workers AI, `env.AI`) as the IN-ACCOUNT OCR path —
  measurement-only, commits no product code, holds no slot, costs nothing to run
  (no account, no credential, no signup).** Against the SAME ground-truthed Oakland
  exhibit and instrument CPDF-9 used, so the numbers are comparable line for line.
  Four things, and the last two carry more weight here than for any classic engine
  because Moondream is GENERATIVE and its failure mode is text that looks better
  than its input:
  (a) **Accuracy** against the measured local-tesseract floor — 99.96% character
  accuracy, 90/90 digits, ZERO minted digits (MEASUREMENTS.md 2026-08-03). Digits
  are where OCR fails and where human checking fails too (DEC-4).
  (b) **Coordinates VERIFIED, not assumed** — the model claims bounding boxes;
  confirm they ALIGN with the text they are returned for, because the image-region
  anchor is what lets a reader check a claim against pixels and an attester testify
  to it. This is the one non-negotiable structural requirement.
  (c) **The blank-page negative control, weighted heavily** — a blank and a
  noise-only page must yield NOTHING, never plausible text. An engine that
  hallucinates on noise is the one failure mode that would put invented text in the
  record.
  (d) **The degradation ladder — this is the new measurement and it decides the
  confidence question.** Feed progressively degraded regions of the ground-truthed
  page and score whether the model REFUSES (says illegible / returns nothing) or
  INVENTS. Only measured refusal-reliability licenses structured self-refusal as a
  per-region trigger. **Do NOT ask the model for a confidence score and threshold
  it** — a self-reported number treated as calibrated is the costs-nothing-to-
  produce class and is FORBIDDEN by DEC-35.
  Record in `MEASUREMENTS.md` with date and instrument; state the recommended
  transcription-fidelity CAP for this engine (DEC-4: no machine mints the grade, and
  the measurement sets the ceiling).
behind-interface: none — it commits no code
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `MEASUREMENTS.md` carries character accuracy, digit accuracy, minted-
  digit count, coordinate-alignment verdict, blank/noise-page result and the
  degradation-ladder refuse-vs-invent table — each with date and instrument — plus a
  stated recommended cap and a GO/NO-GO on the in-account path; negative control —
  the blank page and the noise page, run and reported, with any non-empty output
  reported as a FAILURE of the path rather than a curiosity.
added: 2026-08-04 · BOB
landed: (merge on main, worker 9d6274a) — model @cf/moondream/moondream3.1-9B-A2B, reached through a scratch Worker carrying env.AI (the project token has no Workers AI REST scope — measured, 403 vs 200), uploaded/used/DELETED-and-verified-gone; account pinned before any upload; nothing funded, ~$0.019 of tokens. VS THE FLOOR: 99.44% char (floor 99.96%), 89/90 digits (the miss an OMISSION of a page number, not a misread), 0 minted at full resolution — and NOT REPRODUCIBLE (3 runs of identical bytes, 2 distinct transcriptions). COORDINATES: FAIL, verified not assumed — 2 of 24 box-checks align, task=query returns no boxes with text, and it returned a CONFIDENT box for $99,999,999.99 which is not on the page. CONTROLS PASS (blank 3/3 + noise 3/3 → ILLEGIBLE, zero non-empty). THE LADDER'S SHAPE: refusal is a FLOOR ALARM ONE RUNG PAST THE INVENTION BAND, not a boundary alarm — R3 (75dpi+blur) refused 0/3 with 16–20 MINTED digits per run in fluent prose indistinguishable from a clean run ($50,000 → $10,000; 'lowest responsible bidder' → 'least responsible'), R4 refused 3/3. So measured refusal-reliability does NOT license structured self-refusal; the cap carries everything. CAP: C, NEVER B (non-reproducible; no checkable region; R3 indistinguishable from R0) + a rule the cap does not reach: a digit from this engine may not carry a leg without member attestation. VERDICT: NO-GO as the default path on the non-negotiable. THE LIVE ALTERNATIVE, CONDUCT's sequencing ruling at integration: the COMPOSED shape (detect → crop → transcribe the crop) scored 99.62%, 10/10 digits, 0 minted on the one trustworthy region — n=1, UNPROVEN NOT REFUTED, and the ONLY in-account shape that can carry the anchor; it needs the renderer, so closing CPDF-12 and measuring it are ALTERNATIVES — CPDF-12 is RE-SCOPED to the composed-shape measurement (still in DEC-35's in-account-first preference, still nothing funded) and CPDF-10 waits on ITS verdict before the external tier is funded. Two instrument findings kept (both wrong instruments gave the MORE FLATTERING number — the displaced-box criterion measured text density; the referee window was 47% placeable); comparability with CPDF-9 ENFORCED not claimed (the probe imports the floor's ground truth and exits if a metric expression moved). Battery untouched, discovery-rule clean.

### CPDF-12 · done
milestone: M2
scope: **The page-to-pixels renderer — named at last (the substance of two dangling-dependency flags).** Moondream consumes IMAGES, so the in-account OCR route needs a PDF page rendered to pixels; nothing in Workers renders one today (pdf.js needs a canvas — CPDF-9's finding). RE-SCOPED AGAIN 2026-08-04 by DEC-42 (Workers Paid) — TESSERACT FLEET MEMBER FIRST, and it is cheaper than both prior scopes: a DEPLOYED wasm CPU probe walked in reference iterations (CPDF-9's own stated caveat — its node figures are CPU-ORDER only, and a Worker cannot time itself, D-56), plus the MEMORY measurement nobody has taken (a 3300x2550 RGBA frame is 33.6 MB against a 128 MB isolate). Tesseract is the FLOOR every candidate is scored against (99.96% char, 90/90 digits, ZERO minted) and is a classic engine, so it supplies the per-word confidence and coordinates Moondream measurably failed. THE OBSERVATION THAT MAY REMOVE THE RENDERER ENTIRELY, to be VERIFIED not assumed: on the measured exhibit each page is ONE full-page embedded image (JPEG DCTDecode, CCITT G4) — for the image-only class, which is what OCR is FOR, that means image EXTRACTION not page rasterisation; check it across the corpus BEFORE building a renderer, and if it holds the expensive half is a JPEG pass-through plus a CCITT G4 decoder. **AND DEC-41 SEQUENCES WITH IT (2026-08-04): the published container's PDF renderings are this item's other consumer** — the manifest's reserved layout.note join becomes REAL (a server-side renderer produces the PDFs; they join parts[] with their hashes in published_shas), because both needs want page-shaped output from workerd and building two renderers would be the D-164 lesson repeated. Every page carries DEC-34's full header PLUS DEC-40's filter line. The manifest gains an explicit `import_only` flag and the publish ceremony states it BEFORE signing, since it changes what the artifact IS. **REC-22's shipped assertion that no page-shaped artifact is produced in the plane is SUPERSEDED — correct that suite, never exempt it.** The prior scope's composed-shape probe and renderer stay available if tesseract fails its probe. Prior scope, kept as the reasoning that got here: the verdict's composed shape (detect the block → crop → transcribe the crop) is the only in-account shape that can carry the image-region anchor, scored 99.62%/10-of-10/0-minted at n=1, and NEEDS this renderer — so the item is now (a) the renderer measurement-first (bundle/CPU per the CPDF-9 pattern; a fleet member the likely placement, I6) and (b) THE COMPOSED-SHAPE PROBE at n>>1 against the same enforced-comparable floor (reproducibility across runs, minted digits, anchor checkability per crop — the three NO-GO reasons re-measured under the composed shape). Still measurement + instrument; product code only for the renderer if its own numbers permit. CPDF-10 waits on this verdict before the external tier is funded (DEC-35's in-account-first preference, nothing funded).

### CPDF-13 · queued
milestone: M2
scope: **The CALIBRATION construct and its scheduled re-probe (D-183).** (a) A
  calibration is a dated, identified fidelity measurement of a named derivation
  engine+version, stored, with the probe inputs and scores that produced it. (b)
  `text_source`'s chain gains a calibration REFERENCE at each derivation step, so a
  transcription names the measurement its grade rests on. (c) A sixth REC-1 alarm
  consumer re-runs the probe on a cadence (start monthly; the cadence is a declared
  constant, revisable by measurement, and it runs on the INSTANCE'S OWN account —
  one probe per cadence against the free allocation, which the plan must state so
  no group discovers it as a surprise). (d) The ASYMMETRIC drift handler above: a
  worse calibration raises a re-evaluation obligation naming exactly the affected
  transcriptions and re-grades NOTHING; a better one raises nothing. (e) An
  announcement watch is OPTIONAL and may only shorten the interval to the next
  probe — it may never stand IN for one, and it may never itself change a grade.
  Build the construct ENGINE-GENERIC (pdf.js Tier 2 and any future service are
  calibratable by the same shape), not OCR-specific.

### CAP-4 · done
milestone: M2
scope: `CAPTURE-SCALING.md` item 6, DECIDED 2026-07-31 under Bob's delegation — read that item before building, it carries four specifics. (a) Post-hoc reuse verification from `site_assets`, unconditional, costs zero requests, verdicts appended and dated. (b) At ratification, re-fetch every reused part: MANDATORY as an attempt and a record, never as agreement — `confirmed` / `changed` / `unavailable` all ratify, and ratifying with a reused part while saying nothing is what is forbidden. (c) A PLAIN GET, not `If-None-Match`: both cost one subrequest, and a 304 is the origin's assertion where a hash is our own evidence. This deliberately reverses the conditional-GET suggestion elsewhere in that document, which is right for working capture and wrong here. (d) A fourth outcome `not_attempted`, recorded with its reason, for parts the invocation's budget could not reach — bounded by the calibrated ceiling in `capture_limits`, never silently omitted.
behind-interface: none
depends-on: none
accepts-when: a bundle ratified with reused parts records an outcome for every reused part; a source that has gone dark still ratifies, as `unavailable`; a bundle whose reuse count exceeds the calibrated ceiling ratifies with the residue recorded `not_attempted`. Negative control — drop the outcome record and the suite fails naming the unrecorded part.

### FW-7 · done
milestone: M4
scope: CONSTRUCTS Step 4, SLICE B — the RECOGNISERS. Resolve a reading reference (`reading_refs`, FW-5, a raw `kind:key`) to a registry entity (`entities`/`entity_aliases`, FW-6), and DECLARE THE METHOD, which IS the §8.1 connection grade (framework §8.1): **A** = the source's own identifier, both ends captured; **B** = an identifier the source uses, matched exactly in captured content at both ends; **C** = correspondence (a name/title/date proximity), NEVER presented as established and flagged for a member to confirm. Store each resolution (`capture_sha`/reading_ref → `entity_id`, with grade + method) in a new table (I5, additive). This delivers the reverse index — "every document that concerns this entity" — the single largest piece of manual work the framework removes. Undetermined is first-class: a reference matching NO entity stays honestly UNRESOLVED (never force-matched); a Grade C correspondence is flagged for confirmation, never shown as established (an equality that costs nothing is not evidence). A declared relation (FW-6) is constitutive and stays OUTSIDE this grade — do NOT grade relations. Grade is IMPROVABLE (§8.1: a C becomes B when a shared identifier is found) — model the resolution so its grade can be RAISED, not frozen.
behind-interface: I5
depends-on: FW-5, FW-6
accepts-when: a reading reference matching an entity's alias resolves to it at grade C, and one matching a source identifier at both ends resolves at grade A/B; "every document concerning entity X" returns the captures whose readings resolve to X; a reference matching nothing is honestly unresolved; negative control — a Grade C resolution is not reported as established, AND dropping the resolver empties the reverse index for a document known to concern X.

landed: 2d11c03 — the RECOGNISERS: new DERIVED table `resolutions` (capture_sha,ref,entity_id PK; grade,method,basis,established,raised_from). #recognise matches a reading_ref against entity_aliases in strict priority — A composite id, B bare id in content, C name correspondence — records at the strongest tier that hit, never traverses a declared relation (D-83). Grade IMPROVABLE in place (C→A via raised_from; #upsertResolution never downgrades, never duplicates). Grade C STRUCTURALLY can't read established. op=concerns = the reverse index "every document that concerns entity X". op=resolvetestify holds member grade-D testimony (author+date, never machine-minted). 4 ops (resolve/resolvetestify mutating contribute + resolutions/concerns reads). I5 1.3.0 additive. purge covers it (both arms, D-113). battery 58/58, coverage --strict 97/97. NCs RUN (force C established → 3 fail; neuter upsert → reverse index empties). disposition: **RETIRED**, worked on `worktree-agent-a04633fc02d8cd2aa`, NOT pushed and NOT merged — CONDUCT integrates. MEASURED BEFORE DECIDING: `data/citations.json` is written and read by **nothing** in `bio-plane/src`, `civicos-ui`, `docprofile`, `tools`, `agent-worker`, `pdf-worker` or `newgroup/src`, while every sibling register the catalogue gates has a real producer (`data/provenance.json` 9 files, `_history/manifest.json` 4, `data/inbox.json` / `data/dataset.json` 2 each). Driven through `checkBundle` on seven inputs, C-8.1 FIRED on four malformed registers, drew nothing on a conformant one, and fired **ALONE** — the same tamper under any other filename draws nothing, so nothing else was doing its work. **The finding is NOT "redundant" and NOT "cannot fire": it is a check with NO PRODUCER**, which had therefore never fired on anything real. Retired because `inquiry_basis` / `inquiry_basis_versions` now hold the claim layer as rows, and a claim living inside another object's file cannot be cited, contradicted, graded or composed — keeping both would have left two claim structures with no rule saying which is authoritative (D-69's diffusion), which `DATA-MODEL.md` §2.2.2 and `BUILD-ORDER.md` §2 both call for retiring. **CORRECTED, NEVER EXEMPTED:** the check is deleted, the id survives in a new exported `CHECK_RETIREMENTS` table stating what it was for and why the old rule was wrong, `coverage.mjs` therefore still counts it and still demands an assertion naming it, and the assertion naming it is the one PROVING IT NO LONGER FIRES. Three assertions stand behind the retirement — the shape no longer fires (five arms, including the over-strictness arm that keeps a legitimate register legal), no retired id is pushed anywhere in the catalogue, and **the estate grew no producer**, which is this row's own "no two structures claim the same thing". Battery **131/131 · 8,219** (baseline in this worktree before any edit 131/131 · 8,209; delta **+10**, all of it `check-firing.test.mjs` 73 -> 83, no other suite moved); `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` from the repo root exit 0; `plancheck` clean but for UNPUSHED. **NO floor moved and none was invalidated** (no suite added or removed; C-8.1 is a check id and not a refusal code, so the DEC-49 guard's corpus floor of 43 is unmoved). FOUR CONTROL ARMS RUN, each ALONE, **zero behaved other than declared** — re-run in one step: `node test/retirement.control.mjs all` from `bio-plane/`. **SWEPT FOR THE CLASS and found a SECOND member, delegated rather than guessed: `data/deletions.json` / C-7.1 has the identical signature (gated, proven to fire, written by nothing) and needs its own row — the argument that retired C-8.1 does not transfer, because nothing collides with a deletion ledger.** See `CLAIMS.md` 2026-08-08 FRAMEWORK and its DELEGATION.

### FW-15 · done
milestone: M4
scope: **C-7.1 / `data/deletions.json` — THE SAME ORPHAN CLASS FW-13 CLOSED, AND THE ARGUMENT THAT RETIRED ITS SIBLING DOES NOT TRANSFER. Decide it: retire, or BIND.** Found 2026-08-08 by FW-13's class sweep, **which enumerated the whole class rather than fixing the one file it was sent for**: 11 literal bundle paths gated by the catalogue plus 13 built from expressions its matcher could not resolve (listed by line), over 118 files across 7 roots. **Exactly TWO orphans — gated, written by nothing and read by nothing: `data/citations.json` (C-8.1, now RETIRED) and `data/deletions.json` (C-7.1, this row).** **FW-13 EXPLICITLY DECLINED TO GUESS THIS ONE, and its reasoning is the item's starting point: C-8.1 was retired because a SECOND claim structure COLLIDED with `inquiry_basis` — two structures claiming the same thing with no rule saying which is authoritative (D-69's diffusion), the second being the one a member can hand-fill while the plane knows nothing about it. NOTHING COLLIDES WITH A DELETION LEDGER. So the retire argument does not carry, and C-7.1 PLAUSIBLY WANTS BIND** — which makes this a different item and not a repeat. **MEASURE BEFORE DECIDING, exactly as FW-13 did:** does anything produce or consume it; **does the check FIRE, and when it fires is it the check doing the work or something behind it** — driven through `checkBundle` over planted inputs, and driven under a DIFFERENT filename to prove the check is what draws, which is the arm that told FW-13 its check was reachable rather than dead. **And note FW-13's exclusion, or your first run will report zero orphans as its did: `newgroup/src/release.mjs` holds a `RELEASE_SOURCE` string containing a whole copy of the bundled plane**, so a naive corpus walk finds a producer that is a copy of the file it is walking. **WHY BIND IS NOT AUTOMATIC EITHER:** a deletion ledger is a claim about what is NO LONGER THERE, and this record's posture is that **correction moves FORWARD (DEC-19) and a published act is IRREVERSIBLE** — so a binding must say what a deletion ledger MEANS against a record that does not erase, and whether it records a member's act, an operator's, or a purge. **A ledger that reads as *this was removed* over a record that never removes would be the record claiming more than it can support**, which `CLAUDE.md` ranks above a missing feature. **If it binds it needs a producer, a consumer and a check that FIRES** — a shape that is authorable and not actable is the defect REC-72 measured. **Correct the superseded check, never exempt it**, and if you retire it, follow FW-13's shape: **the id survives in `CHECK_RETIREMENTS` stating what it was for, why the old rule was wrong, what supersedes it and the measurement — so `coverage.mjs` still counts it and still demands an assertion naming it, and that assertion is the one proving it NO LONGER FIRES.**
behind-interface: I3 if it binds and publishes; none if it retires
depends-on: FW-13 (landed — read its `CHECK_RETIREMENTS` table, its `retirement.control.mjs`, and its estate walk before writing anything; **do not build a second mechanism for the same job**, which is CPDF-9's rule and how the next component goes dark differently)
accepts-when: **RETIRE or BIND, decided and shipped, with the superseded check CORRECTED and a comment saying why the old one was wrong.** `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline and trust it over this brief** — delta attributed per suite by re-running the true baseline; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0; `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` **from the REPO ROOT**, exit read unpiped, 0; **every floor you invalidate moved IN THE SAME TURN from PRINTED figures** — and heed FW-13's receipt: it first named its table with a `_CHECKS` suffix and **`check-refusal-codes.mjs` harvests every `/_CHECKS$/` export as a DEC-49 family**, which grew a ratchet's floor falsely before the rename. `_CHECKS` is a RESERVED SUFFIX. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded, each armed ALONE, restores verified by sha256 AND content against uniquely-named per-arm copies with a printed byte count and a minimum floor, the harness INSIDE your own worktree — (1) if you RETIRE, **plant the retired thing back and an assertion must FAIL naming it**, or the retirement is a deletion nobody enforces; if you BIND, **break the binding and the check must FAIL**; (2) **grow a producer and the estate arm must name it BY FILE** — FW-13's arm (ii), and it is what keeps *no two structures claim the same thing* true tomorrow; (3) neuter the estate walk and its REACH fails as a DELTA with the corpus PRINTED; (4) an over-strictness arm — a legitimate register must draw NOTHING.
added: 2026-08-08 · CONDUCT (FW-13's delegation, routed at its integration; FW-13 measured the class and declined to guess this member, which is why it is a row rather than a rider)
disposition: **RETIRED**, worked on `worktree-agent-a2c81ec63553ea011`, NOT pushed and NOT merged — CONDUCT integrates. **THIS ROW'S OWN PREMISE IS THE THING THE MEASUREMENT CORRECTED, and it is the finding rather than a footnote.** The row reads *"NOTHING COLLIDES WITH A DELETION LEDGER, so the retire argument does not carry, and C-7.1 PLAUSIBLY WANTS BIND"*. **Something does collide — the mechanism that already discharges all three of State Rules §2.5's gated-deletion requirements at the only write in the plane that can remove anything.** Measured on the running code, not read off a comment: a promotion that drops a file by OMISSION is refused `FILES_DROPPED` naming the path (C-33.24, DEC-49 region `is-promote-files`, with a member translation) = §2.5's *stated reason, never silent*; naming it in `drop[]` succeeds and the removed bytes are then byte-identical at `_history/data/<name>_<snapKey>.json`, carried in that promotion's `snapshotted[]` while absent from its `files[]` = §2.5's *preservation*, with C-12.2 firing on a snapshot that goes missing; every projection is rebuilt in the same transaction so an edge into removed material reads as an unresolvable C-6.2 finding = §2.5's *cascade*, with C-5.1 refusing any append-only surface that shrank. **So `data/deletions.json` was a SECOND ACCOUNT OF AN ABSENCE beside a machine-kept one — D-69's diffusion, the same defect that retired C-8.1, through a different door.** THE OTHER THREE MEASUREMENTS: **no producer** — 0 files write or read it in a 118-file corpus across the seven roots (`data/provenance.json` 9, `_history/manifest.json` 4, `data/inbox.json` and `data/dataset.json` 2 each), `newgroup/src/release.mjs` excluded by name as FW-13 established. **It FIRED and it fired ALONE** — sixteen planted inputs through `checkBundle`: six distinct malformed ledgers drew C-7.1, a well-formed one drew nothing, and the identical tamper under `data/deletionz.json`, `data/removals.json`, `data/deleted.json` and bare `deletions.json` drew NO error at all, so nothing else was doing its work. **Which of the three findings: NEVER FIRED** — not "cannot fire", not "redundant"; a check with no producer. **And it is WORSE than C-8.1's, which settled it:** C-7.1 checked the SHAPE of a deletion claim and nothing about its TRUTH — it passed a ledger naming `bundle.md` as deleted while `bundle.md` was present, a `preserved_to` pointing at a path the bundle did not hold, and records out of order in an append-only ledger. **An absence is exactly the claim a reader cannot check from the bundle**, so the hand-authorable ledger is the record claiming more than it can support. **WHOSE ACT IT WOULD HAVE RECORDED: none that exists.** A member has no delete — correction moves FORWARD (DEC-19) and every form of it is an ADDITION already carried by `state_history`, the `_history/` chain and `inquiry_basis_versions`. An operator's `op=purge` is EVICTION, admin-only, and takes the `history` and `manifest` tables with the bundle, so it can never write a `preserved_to` and destroys the very bundle the ledger would live in. A purge is that same act. Published bytes are exempt from all of it — a hash once published answers forever. CORRECTED, NEVER EXEMPTED: the check is deleted, the id survives in `CHECK_RETIREMENTS` beside C-8.1's with what it gated, why the old rule was wrong, the measurement and what supersedes it, so `coverage.mjs` still counts C-7.1 and still demands the assertion that PROVES IT NO LONGER FIRES. **FW-13's mechanism was WIDENED, not duplicated** (CPDF-9): the retirement block is keyed by retired id and reads ids and gated paths out of the catalogue; the different-filename arm is now STANDING rather than a one-time measurement; a new COMPLETENESS arm fails when a `CHECK_RETIREMENTS` row has no behavioural arms; `retirement.control.mjs`'s per-check arms run once per id. Battery **131/131 · 8,229** (baseline measured in this worktree before any edit **131/131 · 8,219**, which AGREES with the brief exactly; delta **+10**, ALL of it check-firing 83 -> 93, no other suite moved). `coverage --strict` run directly, `$?` unpiped, exit 0 — OPS 159/159, CHECKS 208/208 named. UI harness from the repo root exit 0. **ONE FLOOR MOVED, UPWARD: `REGISTER_FLOOR.arms`, which the baseline already printed as 550/546 because FW-13 grew a declaration and did not collect it.** SIX CONTROL ARM-RUNS, each ALONE, and **ONE CAME BACK WRONG:** the new UNCOVERED arm first reported `THE SUITE NEVER REACHED ITS FOOT` — an unguarded index in my own widening threw a `TypeError` that ended the module through no assertion at all, caught only because FW-13's harness reads the foot line rather than a tally. Corrected at the site with the receipt; re-run and behaved as declared. DELEGATED: `REEVAL_SOURCES`'s `'deletion'` value is §2.5's cascade half and has no writer either — one value of one enum inside a live, producing check, which probably wants a rider on FW-14 rather than a row.

### UI-38 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **The SURFACE REGISTRY and the RECIPE format with their build-time validation — `ASSISTANT-PILOT.md` §7 step 1, and it needs no AI at all.** Bob picked DEC-27's assistant as the first AI integration (2026-08-04) and the design's load-bearing choice is that the assistant's "training" is **SELF-DESCRIPTION, not a document** — five layers ordered by drift rate. This item builds the two that drift fastest, and it pays for itself as documentation the day it lands whether or not an assistant is ever built. (a) **Surfaces SELF-DESCRIBE**: every member-facing surface in `civicos-ui/` carries an id and a machine-readable statement of what it is for and which acts it offers, and **the HARNESS enforces that the described set EQUALS the real set** — a surface that exists and describes nothing, and a description naming a surface that does not exist, must each FAIL the build. (b) **Recipes are DATA, never prose**: a recipe is an ordered list of steps whose steps NAME surface ids and plane ops, and **the build FAILS when a step names a surface or an op that does not exist** — the D-106 defence applied to assistant knowledge, and the entire reason this is worth building before any AI exists. Take the op names from **the registry the plane already EMITS** (that layer cannot drift), never from a hand-typed list: **a hand copy agrees at zero cost and this project has measured that five times on five subjects**, so the drift assertion must be STRUCTURAL. Two constraints from the design that a build session would otherwise lose: the ~305 teaching-grade refusal `detail:` strings are the answer key and are surfaced VERBATIM, never paraphrased; and an answer reporting absence must name its LEVEL (meaning / content / documents / internet), so a surface's self-description must make its level legible rather than leaving it to the caller. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not whether the surfaces you start with are described, it is whether a NEW one can be added silently.

**SCOPE AMENDED 2026-08-07 by CONDUCT, enacting the investigative-session handover — and this is the collision `IS-SWEEP-2026-08-07.md` §5a flagged, which would have been expensive to discover late.** `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14a says **the running-session surface is designed ONCE for all AI features.** UI-38 was already building the assistant's surface registry, so left as it stood **two AI features would have grown two surfaces** — the mirror-and-drift class arriving at the UI layer. **So UI-38 ABSORBS the once-only running-session surface (E10 of `FINDINGS-WORKPLAN.md`),** and builds it as the single surface both the assistant pilot and the investigative session appear on. **It also carries F11 from §19, which is a finding rather than a preference: the budget is RECORDED and never SHOWN** — §14b.6 bounds effort and logs it, but there is no member-facing budget at launch and no live spend while a session runs. The surface must carry the budget and its live consumption, **since which account pays is already named on the record**, and a bound that a member can only read afterwards is not a bound they can act on. Two constraints that are not yours to decide here: the run's *content* is IS-6's and IS-9's, so build the SURFACE and read what those publish rather than deriving state; and **do not author member-facing wording DEC-49 would decide** — lift what the record publishes, the way UI-39 deleted a falsehood without writing a new word and UI-40 rendered the plane's accounts verbatim with no fallback.
behind-interface: I3
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` green with every surface described and the described set proved equal to the real set **in both directions**; at least one authored recipe whose every step resolves to a real surface id and a real EMITTED op, with the op names read from the plane's own registry and that sourcing asserted (a hand-typed list must fail the assertion); `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` stays green — 100/100 and 5,664 today, any delta attributed per suite; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY with `$?` read unpiped, exit 0. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded: (1) a recipe step naming an op the plane does not emit FAILS the build, naming the step AND the op; (2) a recipe step naming a surface id that does not exist FAILS naming it; (3) add a real surface, describe it nowhere, and the harness FAILS; (4) **neuter the walk that discovers surfaces and the REACH assertion fails as a DELTA** — a walk that covers nothing passes everything, and that class has eight sightings this week including one instrument that compared a planted count to 1; (5) the pins must go RED when a described-but-absent surface is introduced and GREEN when it is removed, not the reverse.

What is DECLARED versus BORROWED is the drift defence — routes checked against a walk and never trusted; `acts` checked against the plane's **live-imported** catalogue so no copy of it exists in the runtime; `reads` against the parsed `OPS` table AND a call-shaped occurrence. **THE REAL SET IS WALKED BY THREE INDEPENDENT ARMS** (`go()`'s screen table, `CUR = {type:…}` object pages, hash-route regexes) **because a surface enters three structurally different ways — and non-redundancy is PROVED: the hash arm alone contributes `published` and `case`, so a one-armed walk would miss them SILENTLY.** Recipes are data and **"never prose" is asserted to also mean "never code"** (JSON round-trip clean): 3 recipes, 9 steps, each naming a real surface id and a real emitted op. **THE TWO HALVES SHARE ONE SURFACE, AND IT IS ENFORCED RATHER THAN PROMISED:** the running-session surface is an entry in the same registry walked by the same arms, carrying `kind:"ai-session"` with both `assistant` and `investigative-session` as consumers, and **ARM X1 asserts there is EXACTLY ONE surface of that kind — a second AI feature growing a second surface FAILS THE BUILD.** That single assertion is what makes §14a's "designed once" a mechanism. **F11 CARRIED: the surface renders the budget, its live consumption and the account paying, and DERIVES NOTHING** — the pin is over RENDERED OUTPUT, so a percentage introduced by any route at all fails; renderers are field-name-blind so IS-6/IS-9 may pick any shape; it names NO op (none publishes a run yet) and **no record means NO INDICATOR rather than an invented "nothing is running"**. UI harness exit 0 read unpiped, **35 → 36 harnesses**, `surface-registry` at 359 assertions. `--strict` run directly, exit 0. **NCs RUN x11, all RED with the defect and GREEN after restore**, including each of the three walk arms neutered as a delta with the corpus printed, a second `ai-session` surface, a derived percentage, and a plane fetch for the transcript. **THREE FINDINGS FROM ITS OWN RUN, each recorded at the site:** ARM A4 failed on first run and **the worker's DESCRIPTION was wrong rather than the plane** (the plane publishes `publish` and no described surface hosted it — found in `openInquiry`); ARM P5 failed on the worker's own recipe, because **a "FIND" whose surfaces reach no level could never honestly report an absence** — relabelled with the reason at the site; and adding the router without adding it to `boot()`'s chain **would have repeated a defect recorded in a comment three lines above it.** **TWO INSTRUMENT FAULTS BEYOND THE SOURCING ONE:** an arm printed "0 routes — bundle, inquiry, project, action", a count contradicting its own evidence list; and an arm asserted `length + 26` for a 23-character suffix — **a hand-typed constant wrong by three, inside the arm whose subject is that hand-typed constants are wrong.** **AND ONE THAT CHANGES HOW EVERY WORKER MUST RESTORE: an earlier NC harness reported a BYTE-IDENTICAL RESTORE over a file that had NOT been restored** — all restores are now verified by CONTENT as well as sha256. **CONDUCT RE-RAN ARM X1 ITSELF** (it guards the collision CONDUCT amended this scope to prevent): adding a second `ai-session` surface makes the harness exit NON-ZERO with `surface-registry` failing; `app.html` restored and verified by BOTH sha256 and content, per this item's own lesson.
