landed: (merge on main db82819, worker 7e0a5c3 + 1e5d5b0) — **(a) WAS A WRITE, AND IT WAS WRITING A FALSEHOOD TOO.** `op=search` was called with no `limit`, so the plane answered at `LIMIT_DEFAULT` 50 and published `total` into nothing; `addGo` decides on that read whether to call `op=promote`. **A match past row 50 at an address was invisible, so the record grew a SECOND BUNDLE for one document** — and because `CHANGED_FROM` stayed null it grew one carrying NONE of the "a second capture rather than a repeat of the first" sentence. **C-18.3's ring-once rule cannot catch this: it compares hashes WITHIN a single bundle.** The address arm now walks the whole answer the plane counted, paging on `total` and **advancing by what the plane SERVED rather than what was asked, so a clamp cannot make it skip rows**; `null` no longer does double duty (it meant *not found* while the write path read it as *not held*), and a bounded walk returns `{bounded, examined, total}` whose bound travels **into the BUNDLE BODY** by the same mechanism `CHANGED_FROM` uses — *a bound stated in a progress line the next repaint erases is not stated*. **THE WRITE IS PROVED, NOT THE DISPLAY:** ARM A drives the REAL `addGo` end to end against a wire mock, plants the match at row 600 of 700, asserts `op=promote` NEVER REACHES THE WIRE, **and asserts the trap is ARMED** (the match is provably absent from the page an unpaged read saw, past both `LIMIT_DEFAULT` and `LIMIT_MAX`, so it exercises paging rather than a bigger ask) — without that, "no promote" costs nothing. **THE DEFECT THE BRIEF DID NOT NAME, and it is the sharpest: `CHANGED_FROM` was never reset per run** — assigned only inside `if(loc)`, so a TYPED intake inherited the previous add's carry and wrote *"The record already holds an earlier capture of this same address (INFO-…)"* **naming an unrelated bundle. A false sentence about the record, in a document's own body.** One line, inside the claimed function, driven by ARM A2. **A NEW SUB-CLASS: a bound passed by SPREAD is invisible to any wire-anchored walk** — `recR("search", { q, ...extra })` means grepping for `limit` finds the callers while no walk can verify the call site; fixed STRUCTURALLY by making the bound a REQUIRED ARGUMENT of the search helper, which is the difference between a rule and a habit. A FIFTH site (`heldMatch`'s hash arm) was flagged by the sweep against a comment the worker had written arguing it was harmless — **"harmless" is a judgement a sweep cannot check**, so the bound is STATED rather than exempted. **(d) CORRECTED WITHOUT AUTHORING A WORD, which is how DEC-49 stayed unpreempted:** `entitiesByAlias` matches `alias_norm` exactly, so *"a name that is not here has not been entered yet"* is FALSE whenever a subject is registered under a spelling the member did not type; the finder has shipped the true sentence since REC-36 forty screens away, and it is now lifted into ONE function rendered at both sites — the falsehood deleted, the true clause kept, **pinned as a RELATION asserting no wording**, so DEC-49 can rule any way and the pin holds. UI harness exit 0, 35 harnesses (`bound-sweep` new at 49); arm B 833 → **2,477 op answers across 67 ops** (`acquire` newly covered), 152 → 166 pairs. **Battery 101/101 at 5,735 in the worktree with ZERO delta and no `bio-plane/**` file touched** — structural evidence, not a claim. On main after merge: 102/102 at 5,801, the +2 over REC-55's 5,799 attributed to planning-hygiene 241 → 243, this item's two DELEGATION entries, with hygiene unchanged at 429 confirming no plane file moved. `--strict` run directly, exit 0. **NCs RUN ×8**, every file restored byte-identically with sha256 compared, and **THREE of them are instrument findings reported rather than smoothed**: NC-8 went **GREEN FIRST TIME with the defect present** because the fixture never set the carry — rebuilt through the real classifier, then 2 fail; the falsehood-absent pin went RED against the worker's OWN explanatory comments (D-160's shape), resolved by sweeping comment-stripped code with an instrument pin proving the stripper does not strip everything; and a matcher's `[^}]*` stopped at the `}` inside `...(extra||{})`, **reading a bound that was there as missing** — now brace-balanced. **CONDUCT RE-RAN THE WRITE CONTROL ITSELF** (this changes what the record holds): stripping the walk to a single page → **8 fail, the FIRST being "THE WRITE IS PREVENTED: a match past the plane's default produces NO op=promote"** — 8 against the worker's 9 because CONDUCT armed a single page where the worker armed a fully unpaged read, a difference in arming rather than in the guard. ### UI-52 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **`surface-registry`'s ARM A4 asserts a property that only holds at WAVE COMPLETION, and it is blocking a finished plane item — narrow the ASSERTION, do not weaken the guard and do not build the surfaces early.** PL-2 publishes six member acts through `op=affordances`; ARM A4 requires **every published act to be hosted by a described surface**, and the six have none, so the harness goes red. **PL-2's own judgement was RIGHT and is not to be overturned: describing a surface that does not exist would be the registry LYING**, which is the failure ARM A4 exists to catch. **But `IS-BUILD-PLAN.md` builds PLANE-FIRST and schedules the matching UI surfaces three waves later, so during a plane-first wave the property ARM A4 asserts is FALSE BY DESIGN** — and an instrument that fails on correct work gets switched off, which is the outcome this item exists to prevent. **CONDUCT DECIDED THIS RATHER THAN RAISING IT, on a precedent from this same session** (recorded here so it is not re-opened): **REC-71 met exactly this shape** — an assertion whose scope overstated what its rows meant — **and the answer was to narrow the assertion, never to weaken the guard and never to do the neighbouring work early.** **THE SHAPE, and REC-70's lesson is the other half of it:** split the two claims ARM A4 currently makes into one that is always a lie and one that is a tracked, transient gap. **(a) A DESCRIBED SURFACE THAT DOES NOT EXIST stays an unconditional failure** — that is fiction and has no honest window. **(b) A PUBLISHED ACT WITH NO SURFACE YET becomes a NAMED, RATCHETED list** — printed in full every run, with a **CEILING so it cannot grow silently** and a **FLOOR so the walk cannot go blind**, because *a ceiling alone passes trivially over nothing* (REC-70) and *a floor with slack is not a ratchet* (REC-71 — its census floor sat 19 codes low and had already flipped a control from RED to GREEN). **The gap must be VISIBLE rather than tolerated:** the point is that a member-facing act nobody can reach is a real debt, and the list is what makes UI-42/UI-43/UI-45 arrive against a measured obligation instead of a memory.

milestone: M0
scope: **`scripts/coverage.mjs` can report that a suite declares NO negative control when it declares an elaborate one — and can record only the first arm of one that has five (found by REC-48).** The control detector is `/NEGATIVE CONTROL:\s*(.+?)\s*(?:\*\/|$)/` run against `src.split("\n").slice(0, 60).join("\n")`. **CORRECTED 2026-08-04 AT THIS ITEM'S OWN LANDING — MY RE-MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG AND REC-48'S ORIGINAL REPORT WAS RIGHT.** I wrote here that `\s*` crosses newlines so a wrapped control still matches, and that the 60-line window was what bit REC-48. These suites carry long doctrine headers, so when REC-48 made its control block longer the marker crossed line 60 and the strict run went from exit 0 to exit 1 at 96 of 98, naming two suites whose controls had just got BETTER. Measured directly: marker on line 59 with content on line 61 → no match; marker past line 60 → no match. And a second, quieter defect in the same line: `(.+?)` captures only the FIRST line, so a five-arm control is recorded as one arm — the instrument reads as fully green while stating a fraction of what it checked. That is the generous direction, which is the one failure this instrument exists to prevent. FIX: find the declaration wherever it is in the file, capture the whole block, and assert the instrument's own reach — the same reach discipline this project has now needed four times (UI-30, REC-49, UI-28, REC-48's own first draft). Do NOT paper over it with a convention that the marker line must be self-contained; REC-48 worked around it that way to land, and a convention propping up an instrument is the thing to remove.

### UI-37 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **The public verification surface renders a plane REFUSAL as a substantive negative (D-195, measured by UI-36).** `pubVerify` calls `op=verify` through `apiQ`, which — unlike `rec` — does not throw on `ok:false`. So a refusal envelope has no `published` field, falls to the else branch, and an uncredentialed reader is told **"NOT PUBLISHED. No published part answers to that hash."** when the plane actually declined the request. CONDUCT verified both ends: `op=verify` refuses a malformed hash with `{ok:false, error:"verify requires sha256=<64 lowercase hex>"}` at HTTP 400, `api()` never checks status, and the container row composes its hash as `bundle_sha || ""` — so an empty hash reaches it by a click. **This is the worst shape of defect this project recognises: the record asserting something it does not know, on the one surface whose purpose is "check this without us", where the whole claim is that a stranger can verify without our cooperation.** THE FIX IS A JUDGEMENT and the item does not pre-empt it: either `apiQ` throws on `ok:false` as `rec` does — which changes every public read's error path and must be swept for callers, not assumed — or `pubVerify` reads the refusal itself. **Either way it ADDS plane wording to a pre-authentication surface, which is DEC-49's subject**, so whatever it adds must be named on the landed line AND carried into DEC-49's entry, exactly as UI-36 was required to do. Correct UI-36's refusal assertion when this lands; it is written to pin the DEFECT and says so at the site.
behind-interface: I3
depends-on: none — and it should NOT wait on DEC-49. The ruling decides who OWNS the wording; this decides whether the surface tells a stranger something untrue, which is wrong under either answer.
accepts-when: `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` green with a refused `op=verify` rendering as a REFUSAL and never as "not published", driven for at least the malformed-hash arm (reachable today by a click) and any other refusal `op=verify` can emit — sweep for them in `index.mjs` rather than trusting this scope's count of one; UI-36's defect pin CORRECTED at the site with a dated reason, never exempted; `DEC49_SUBJECT` updated deliberately with every new row named, and the new rows carried into DEC-49's entry; both guards green; if `apiQ` changes, every caller swept and the sweep's own reach asserted as a delta. NEGATIVE CONTROL run and recorded — restore the collapse and the harness fails NAMING the refusal that was rendered as a negative; and separately assert that a genuine not-published answer still reads as not-published, because the two must not collapse the other way either.

landed: (merge on main, worker f51f8ae) — **THREE REFUSALS, NOT ONE; THREE SITES, NOT ONE — and the worst was not the one I routed.** `pubList` read `(r && r.published) || []`, so a refusal rendered as **"This group has not published any case files yet"** — a claim about the ENTIRE record, strictly worse than the per-hash one this item was written for. `pubOpen` printed `<h1>Not published</h1>` over every refusal while reading only `detail`, dropping the words of the two arms that carry `error`. The refusals themselves are three: the malformed hash, an **unknown op** (a surface deployed ahead of its plane — and civicos-ui's deploy is explicitly ungated, so that is reachable in practice), and an empty success. **THE FIX WAS DECIDED ON EVIDENCE AND IT IS NOT THE ONE THE ITEM LEANED TOWARD:** `apiQ` is UNCHANGED and `pubVerify` reads the refusal itself, because `index.mjs` §7a answers `json({ ok: true, ...out.result }, 200)` **without checking `out.ok`** — so a Durable Object failure arrives as `{ok:true}` at HTTP 200 with nothing in it and NO `ok:false` for any seam to throw on. A throwing `apiQ` would have sailed straight past the very case that motivated the item. So each site takes a **positive shape test** (`published === true` / `=== false` / otherwise not answered), and `apiQ`'s three callers keep their own error paths, with its body pinned verbatim and its **caller set pinned as a delta**. **THE HONEST HALF SURVIVED**: the clause "a hash that was never ratified and a hash that never existed are the same answer here, deliberately" is MOVED to the branch where it is true and pinned verbatim there — and the NOT-ANSWERED branch says only what the surface observed (the state of the EXCHANGE), renders the plane's own words where there are any, and falls through to the same fallback the catch branch uses where there are none, **rather than minting a second sentence for the same silence**. A SIXTH state was added that nobody would have thought to ask for — the TRUE NEGATIVE, a well-formed hash the record genuinely does not hold — and arm (q) shows that collapse is one character away in the other direction. **THREE PINS CORRECTED, and two are findings in their own right.** UI-36's refusal arm — written to pin the defect and saying so — had **every requirement backwards**: it would have gone red for the fix and green for the bug. The not-published arm asserted `VERIFY_MATCHES.size === 3`, **a statement about the FIXTURE rather than the surface**; it drove nothing and would have survived a collapse in the opposite direction. And `auth-surface`'s NEG-CONTROL (a) required the broken surface to say "has not published any case files yet" — so **D-173's "honest-looking blank screen" was literally this defect's sentence: the two defects are one rendering**, which is the kind of unification that only turns up when somebody drives the thing. DEC49_SUBJECT: **11 rows → 11**, one new SOURCE on one existing term (`sha256` at `#v-refused`, the plane's own refusal rendered instead of swallowed), nothing vanished. **AND THE PLANE WORDING THE INSTRUMENT CANNOT PIN IS ITEMISED AT THE SITE RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE FOUND**: the `unknown op` refusal now renders on THREE pre-auth surfaces and the store's `NOT_PUBLISHED` sentence renders whole at a case address — neither contains any of the 74 inherited terms nor trips either structural rule. Also self-reported: `NOT_PUBLISHED`, the bare wire code, **briefly did** reach the subject when the first selector joined `reason` with `detail`; the guard caught it and it was corrected to prefer prose, which is UI-30's rule holding two items later. Harness 34/34 exit 0 (preauth-vocabulary 48 → 58, auth-surface 73 → 74); both guards green with the seam set UNCHANGED and arm B's +3 attributed exactly; coverage strict run directly, exit 0; **battery measured BOTH WAYS rather than assumed — 98/98 at 5,544 with the change and 98/98 at 5,544 with it stashed** — and the brief's stale "5541" corrected against the worktree's own baseline. Basis 11 → 15 scenarios, 19 → 22 surfaces, 13 terms unchanged; **the four new scenarios move no number in the report, measured with them hidden.** NC RUN x6 with app.html restored byte-identically: (n) the collapse restored → 4 of 58 naming all three refusals plus the subject arm, while the true-negative arm stays green; (q) the other direction → 1 of 58 **plus a second suite**; (o) the index collapse → 2 fails in two suites; (s) the DEC-8 overstep → 2. **Arm (r) was corrected mid-run and reported rather than smoothed**: it first reverted to `HEAD`, which stopped being the pre-item state the moment this item's own commit landed, and fell silently from 4/54 to 1/54. **REC-52 ROUTED** — two plane-side defects of the same class found in passing.
